\section{Selected principal factors and the lower contour region}
All the following assertions have proofs in the answer, not only here.
In the principal row let \(D=uQ^{-s}\), \(W=Q^{-w}\),
\(\mathcal E=B_Q+D\), and define
\[
 G_Q=\frac{(D^{-1}-W)(1-V)(1-W)B_Q-W(1-VW)}{1-D}.
\]
This is source (16.2), with no division by \(P_p\) or \(H_p\).
The exact identity
\[
 G_Q+H_Q=(1-W)\left(H_Q-1+\frac{V-D}{1-D}\right)
       +\frac{(1-V)(1-W)\mathcal E}{D(1-D)}
\]
and the earlier local-product proof give, throughout
\(\Re s\ge43/50,\ \Re w\ge19/20,\ \Re z\ge33/200\),
\[
 |\mathcal E/D|\le12Q^{-129/100}+8Q^{-47/50},\quad
 |G_Q+H_Q|\le1008Q^{-43/50}.
\]
For \(Q>10^{10}\), \(|H_Q|\ge1/2\), so
\(|G_Q/H_Q+1|\le2048Q^{-43/50}\).  All bounds are uniform in
imaginary parts and unit phases.

The full tuple correction is therefore holomorphic on this enlarged
principal region and is \(O_{\rm fixed}(Z^{\ell\Re z})\).
At the residue its exact factorization is
\[
 \mathfrak H_{\eta,1,Z}(s,1,1/6)
 =H_\eta(s)Z^{\ell/6}A_T(Z)(1+\mathcal R_{\eta,Z}(s)),
 \qquad
 \mathcal R_{\eta,Z}=O_{\rm fixed}(Z^{-\mu}),\quad
 \mu=(43/50)\min_i\ell_i.
\]
Here \(A_T=(-1)^KZ^{-\ell/6}\prod S_i\), and fixed-ray prime
counting gives \(|A_T|\asymp(\log Z)^{-K}\).
Nonnegative slot weights are essential for the relative error.
The fixed ray prime result is source Lemma 13.1 (not 13.2).

For \(B>\theta_{\rm alg}>43/50\), the principal integral in
source (16.7) can now be treated on \(\Re s=B+e\), even if
\(B<7/8\), without clipping.  The \(w,z\) residue paths are unchanged:
\(w:1+e\to19/20\), then in its residue \(z:1/6+e\to33/200\).
The relative errors before small losses are
\[
 (1+h)e-l_y/20,\qquad e-h/600,\qquad e-\mu .
\]
They are measured relative to \(C(B)\), not \(C(B^\sharp)\).
The reciprocal is holomorphic on \(\Re s>B\) by the definition of
\(B\), even when \(B=1\); a principal target pole becomes a reciprocal
zero.  Fixed-target polynomial growth follows from the zero-free
logarithm and Borel--Carath\'eodory.  The independent height coordinates
are \((\Im(s+z),\Im z,\Im w)\), giving absolute integrability and
vanishing horizontal-face integrals from the three tests.

This resolves the principal-residue scale issue raised by the idea of
using \(B^\sharp=\max(B,7/8)\): there is no need to require
\(\lambda/4<\mu\).  Once the slots are fixed, take \(e\) below the
three positive principal margins.  Only nonprincipal contour estimates
might still be carried out with the supplied ceiling \(B^\sharp\).
This conclusion does not establish the nonprincipal moments or the
full high comparison.

\section{Uniform ideal high margins on the enlarged range}
This section isolates the affine model.  Its actual-count hypotheses
are supplied conditionally under \(\mathsf A\) in the later count derivation.
Let \(B\in(\theta_{\rm alg},1]\),
\(\Delta_+=\max(B-7/8,0)\), and let \(\widehat E_\lambda(d;\delta,q,R)\)
be the affine expression from the endpoint section with free \(q,R\).
Assume the following ideal envelope choices.
\begin{enumerate}
\item On \(1/2\le d\le h\), \(1/50<\delta\le5/6\), use
\(q=x\delta\), \(R=R_*+\Delta_+/4\).
The coefficient of \(R\) is \(d\ge0\), and the resulting slope in \(d\)
is positive because \(R_*\ge1-\delta\).  Hence
\[
 \widehat E_\lambda(d)-(B-\theta_{\rm alg})
 \le \widehat E_\lambda(h)-(B-\theta_{\rm alg})
 <-1/10000.
\]
For \(B\ge7/8\), the last left side is at most
\[
 -\frac{59873}{550800000}
   -(1-h/4)\Delta_+-\lambda/4.
\]
For \(B<7/8\), the capacity increment is zero and the positive
\(B-\theta_{\rm alg}\) is subtracted.
\item At the floor \(\delta=1/50\), put \(R=1\), \(q\le\delta/2\).
The slope is positive and the endpoint is
\(-7/1200+(32/25)\lambda<-1/10000\).
\item For \(d\le1/2\), \(\delta\le5/6\), use
\(R=76/75-2\delta/3\), \(q\le\delta/2\).
The slope is \(101/150-\delta/6>0\), and
\[
 \widehat E_\lambda(1/2)
 \le-\frac{529}{2400}+\frac{25\delta}{96}
       +\lambda\left(\frac{13}{50}+\frac{3\delta}{4}\right)
 \le-\frac{49}{14400}+\frac{177\lambda}{200}
 <-1/10000.
\]
\item For \(5/6\le\delta\le1\), use \(R=1-\delta\),
\(q\le\delta/2\).  The earlier unbounded-witness calculation gives
the candidate count; the high slope is positive and the endpoint is
\(-1/48-\delta/16+5\lambda/4<-7/100\).
\end{enumerate}
All these chosen \(R\)'s have slope less than two:
in the adaptive case \(R_*\le1\), \(\Delta_+\le1/8\), and in
the other cases the assertion is immediate.
Thus the extension \(h<d\le h+\zeta\), \(\zeta=10^{-6}\),
costs at most \(2\zeta\), leaving a margin exceeding \(1/10000\)
even in the smallest-margin case, since
\[
 \frac{59873}{550800000}-\frac2{10^6}
   =\frac{293857}{2754000000}>\frac1{10000}.
\]
The slot supply stays \(>1/5>7/37\), because
\(5\ell-h=1/48+7\lambda/2>\zeta\).
Small rows have ideal relative exponent
\(-63/800+(51/100)\lambda\), still strongly negative.
The large-row exponent
\(l_x/2+1+l_y+(h+\zeta)(1+\epsilon)-\zeta z_\infty+\epsilon\)
can be made negative by choosing a fixed \(z_\infty\) sufficiently large.
These are exact algebraic inequalities over continuous ranges;
the actual envelope hypotheses hold under the explicitly retained
arithmetic moment inputs.

\section{Order of height choices}
This reproves the analytic step of
the baseline continuation criterion,
not its arithmetic hypotheses.  It also explains the precise
uniformity needed.  For example, suppose an error bound has the form
\[
 Z^{B+c-m}(1+Z^\tau)^{A_\eta}
           +Z^{K_\eta-N\tau},\qquad m>0,
\]
with a constant depending on \(\eta,N,\tau\), valid for every
\(N\geq0\) and \(0<\tau\leq\tau_{0,\eta}\), where \(\tau_{0,\eta}>0\).
If \(m\) is common, \(A_\eta\geq0\) and \(K_\eta\) are fixed
\emph{before} \(N\), and the compared functions do not depend on
\(N,\tau\), choose
\[
 0<\tau\leq\min\{\tau_{0,\eta},m/[4(A_\eta+1)]\},\qquad
 K_\eta-N\tau<B+c-m/2.
\]
Both terms are then \(O_\eta(Z^{B+c-m/2})\).
This does not establish such an error bound; it isolates the
order-of-choices requirement.


\section{Literature scope}
On 8 October 2026 a web search for improvements of the seven-eighths
quasi-Riemann result found the release descriptions and the previously
identified tomoto0 parameter exploration, rather than a proved smaller
half-plane.  This limited search is not a priority determination.
The standard Hecke continuation input is Poonen, \emph{Tate's thesis},
Theorem 5.22 and Section 5.11, at
\url{https://math.mit.edu/~poonen/786/notes.pdf}.
It concerns the completed function; removing the archimedean gamma factor
gives the finite \(L\)-function in the half-plane used here.
No asserted formal-verification result from another revision is used
to certify any theorem at the pinned revision.
\section{An unchanged low exponent does not descend}
\paragraph{An unchanged low bound is insufficient.}
The baseline's low exponent is \(3/16=C(7/8)\), where
\(C(s)=s-11/16\).  To see why improving only the high error cannot
suffice, set \(J_0=f_0\), where
\[
 f_0(Z)=\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(2)}
   Z^{s-11/16}\frac{e^{(s-5/6)^2}}{s-7/8}\,ds .
\]
Moving to \(\Rea s=3/4\), Gaussian decay kills the horizontal joins
and the single residue gives
\(f_0(Z)=e^{1/576}Z^{3/16}+O(Z^{1/16})\) for \(Z\geq1\).
Thus the low bound is \(O(Z^{3/16})\) and the high error is zero,
despite the pole of the reciprocal at \(7/8\).
Moving right to any \(A>2\) gives \(O_A(Z^{A-11/16})\) near zero.
This countercheck concerns the comparison mechanism, not a Dirichlet
\(L\)-function.

\section{Exact script preserved in full}
The script \texttt{audit\_checks.py} makes no network requests.
It verifies exact identities and rational constants, not zero locations.
\begin{verbatim}
"""Exact checks for the algebraic partial results; NOT an L-function proof.

Run: python audit_checks.py
Requires sympy. No network calls. All assertions use rational/polynomial arithmetic.
The analytic arguments and continuous-domain sign proofs appear in answer.tex.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import json
import sympy as S
BASE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
R = S.Rational
delta,x,lam,d,y = S.symbols("delta x lam d y", real=True)
alpha=R(5,6)
D=3-R(17,9)*x
P=(2-R(8,9)*x)*(1-x)
J=(alpha-delta)*D+delta*P
Rs=1-delta+(alpha-delta)*delta*P/(2*J)
ell=R(1,6)+lam
b=R(1,8)
lx=R(17,48)-lam/2
ly=R(23,48)-lam/2
h=R(13,16)+3*lam/2
th=R(7,8)-lam/4
M=1-ell
z0=R(17,50)
a=(1+delta)/2
q=x*delta
def E(dval, r=Rs, qval=q):
    return a-th+h*(z0-R(1,6))-a*ly-ell/2+qval*ell+dval*(r+delta/2-z0)
assert S.simplify(lx+ly+ell-1)==0
assert S.simplify(h-(1-lx+ell))==0
assert S.simplify(th-(1-h/6+b/12))==0
assert S.simplify(lx/2-1+h/6+R(11,16))==0
assert S.simplify(th-R(11,16)-(lx/2+b/12))==0

E0=S.factor(E(h).subs(lam,0))
E0_explicit=-R(1,48)+R(2,3)*delta+q/6-R(13,16)*(1-Rs)
assert S.factor(E0-E0_explicit)==0
yJ=S.factor(J.subs(x,R(1,2)-y))
yE=S.factor(E0.subs(x,R(1,2)-y))
v=51+41*y
certificate=(3+5*y)*((4*v*delta-79)**2+49)+4*y*(
    4*v*delta*((1+3*y)*(15+32*y)*delta+9-13*y)+265+3485*y)
assert S.factor(10368*v*yJ*(-yE)-certificate)==0
assert S.factor(D-P-(1+x-R(8,9)*x**2))==0
assert S.factor(E(h)-E0-lam*(-R(1,4)+(1+x)*delta+R(3,2)*Rs))==0

eps=R(1,10**6)
new_margin=R(49,440640)-R(5,2)*eps
assert new_margin==R(59873,550800000)
assert new_margin>R(1,10000)
theta=th.subs(lam,eps)
assert theta==R(3499999,4000000)
assert R(7,8)-theta==R(1,4000000)

assert S.simplify(1+3*(ell-d)-2*(M-2*d)-(5*ell+d-1))==0
assert ell.subs(lam,eps)<R(1,5)
assert S.factor(lx-ell-(R(3,16)-3*lam/2))==0
assert S.factor(ly-ell-R(11,6)*b-(R(1,12)-3*lam/2))==0
assert S.factor(M-2*ell-(R(1,2)-3*lam))==0
assert S.factor(ell/h-R(7,37)-(1272*lam+23)/(111*(24*lam+13)))==0

# Additional exploratory model checks, not analytic estimates.
Efloor=S.factor(E(h,r=1,qval=delta/2).subs(delta,R(1,50)))
assert S.simplify(Efloor-(-R(7,1200)+R(32,25)*lam))==0
Ehalf=S.factor(E(R(1,2),r=R(76,75)-R(2,3)*delta,qval=delta/2))
assert S.factor(Ehalf-(-R(529,2400)+R(25,96)*delta
                       +lam*(R(13,50)+R(3,4)*delta)))==0
small=S.factor(h*(z0-R(1,6))-ly/2+R(2,100))
assert S.simplify(small-(-R(63,800)+R(51,100)*lam))==0

# Rational local-factor identities.
V,W,D0,R0,T,Q=S.symbols("V W D0 R0 T Q")
Pstar=((R0*(1-1/Q)-T*V)/(1-V)-D0+W*R0)/(1-R0)
H=(1/(1-V)+W/(1-W)+Pstar)*(1-V)*(1-W)/(1-D0)
E_local=Pstar+D0
E_simplified=(R0*((1-1/Q)/(1-V)+W-D0)-T*V/(1-V))/(1-R0)
H_defect=(D0*(V+W-V*W)-V*W+(1-V)*(1-W)*E_local)/(1-D0)
assert S.factor(E_local-E_simplified)==0
assert S.factor(H-1-H_defect)==0
sr,wr,zr=R(43,50),R(19,20),R(33,200)
decays={
 "R":6*sr+6*zr-4,
 "TV":sr+wr+6*zr-1,
 "DV":sr+6*zr,
 "DW":sr+wr,
 "VW":6*zr+wr
}
assert decays=={"R":R(43,20),"TV":R(9,5),"DV":R(37,20),
               "DW":R(181,100),"VW":R(97,50)}
assert all(e>=R(9,5) for e in decays.values())
assert 336*R(225,4)==18900
local_sum=R(18900,10**8)
assert local_sum==R(189,10**6)
assert local_sum<R(1,4)
assert 2*local_sum<R(1,2)
assert (R(7,8)-R(5,6))**2==R(1,576)

# Reflected-energy identities including small signed dyadic offsets.
ell0,d0,A0,N0,S0,G0,zA,O0,H0,v0,t0,e0,thetaN,u0 = S.symbols(
    "ell0 d0 A0 N0 S0 G0 zA O0 H0 v0 t0 e0 thetaN u0", real=True)
Mp=1-ell0-2*d0
lp=ell0-d0
Gamma=Mp-O0-H0
Td=2*H0+2*A0+2*zA-1-lp-thetaN-N0-3*G0
ys=v0+3*t0+e0
width_rhs=H0-Mp+2*A0+2*(zA-lp)-N0-3*G0-thetaN
assert S.expand(Td-(H0-3*d0)-width_rhs)==0
saving_rhs=(2*Mp+2*zA-1-lp+2*Gamma-thetaN+
            2*A0-N0+G0+4*S0)/4
assert S.expand(O0/2+Gamma+S0+G0+Td/4-saving_rhs)==0
assert S.expand(u0+t0+2*e0/3-ys/4-
                ((u0-v0/2)+v0/4+t0/4+5*e0/12))==0
assert S.expand(1+3*lp-2*Mp-(5*ell0+d0-1))==0
assert R(1,2)+R(1,4)+R(1,4)+R(5,12)==R(17,12)
branch1=7+R(5,3)+R(1,2)+1
branch2=7+R(17,12)+R(3,4)
assert branch1==R(61,6)
assert branch2==R(55,6)
assert max(branch1,branch2)<11

# Norm-lift local factor at a rational inert prime.
u_inert=S.symbols("u_inert")
assert S.expand((1-u_inert)*(1+u_inert)-(1-u_inert**2))==0


# Large-bin bypass: independent algebra, not an arithmetic row-count claim.
ELarge=S.factor(E(h,r=1-delta,qval=delta/2))
assert S.factor(ELarge-(-R(1,48)-delta/16+R(5,4)*lam))==0
large_margin=R(7,96)-R(5,4)*eps
assert large_margin>R(7,100)
r_inverse=S.symbols("r_inverse", real=True)
assert S.factor((1+5*r_inverse)/6-delta*r_inverse-(1-delta)
                -(R(5,6)-delta)*(r_inverse-1))==0
DD,mm,qq=S.symbols("DD mm qq", real=True)
capacity_diff=2*qq*(1-2*mm)*(R(2,9)-1/(R(9,2)+12*DD))
capacity_exact=24*qq*(1-2*mm)*DD/(R(9,2)*(R(9,2)+12*DD))
assert S.factor(capacity_diff-capacity_exact)==0


# Principal selected factor identity and absolute constants.
Gsel=((1/D0-W)*(1-V)*(1-W)*Pstar-W*(1-V*W))/(1-D0)
selected_rhs=(1-W)*(H-1+(V-D0)/(1-D0))+(1-V)*(1-W)*E_local/(D0*(1-D0))
assert S.factor(Gsel+H-selected_rhs)==0
selected_decays={"R_over_D":5*sr+6*zr-4, "TV_over_D":wr+6*zr-1}
assert selected_decays=={"R_over_D":R(129,100),"TV_over_D":R(47,50)}
assert min(selected_decays.values())>=sr
selected_raw=2*(336+8)+16*20
assert selected_raw==1008
assert 2*selected_raw==2016<2048
assert R(336,10**18)<R(1,2) # local H unit for Q>10^10
assert 2048**5<10**43 # selected multiplier magnitude <2

# Principal contour powers; all identities exact for perturbed lengths.
Bceil,ee=S.symbols("Bceil ee",real=True)
C=lambda z:z-R(11,16)
raw=lambda sr0,wr0,zr0:lx/2+sr0-1+h*zr0+ly*(wr0-1)
assert S.factor(raw(Bceil+ee,R(19,20),R(1,6)+ee)
                -(C(Bceil)+(1+h)*ee-ly/20))==0
assert S.factor(raw(Bceil+ee,1,R(33,200))-(C(Bceil)+ee-h/600))==0
assert S.factor(lx/3-R(5,6)+ell/6+R(11,16))==0
assert S.factor(lx/2+b/12-R(3,16)+lam/4)==0
assert (lx/2+b/12).subs(lam,eps)==R(749999,4000000)

# Extended ideal high envelopes (still not arithmetic row counts).
DH=S.symbols("DH",nonnegative=True)
high_plus=S.factor(E(h,r=Rs+DH/4)-(DH+lam/4))
high_plus_expected=E(h)-lam/4-(1-h/4)*DH
assert S.factor(high_plus-high_plus_expected)==0
floor_margin=R(7,1200)-R(32,25)*eps
mid_margin=R(49,14400)-R(177,200)*eps
small_margin=R(63,800)-R(51,100)*eps
assert min(floor_margin,mid_margin,small_margin,large_margin,new_margin)>R(1,10000)
extension=R(1,10**6)
assert new_margin-2*extension>R(1,10000)
assert S.factor(5*ell-h-(R(1,48)+7*lam/2))==0

output={
 "primary_target_proved":False,
 "zeta_only_improvement_proved":False,
 "scope":"Exact algebraic certificates and local rational-factor checks only",
 "geometry_perturbation":str(eps),
 "model_boundary_not_zero_free_constant":str(theta),
 "model_boundary_difference_from_7_over_8":str(R(7,8)-theta),
 "uniform_endpoint_model_margin":str(new_margin),
 "margin_excess_over_1_over_10000":str(new_margin-R(1,10000)),
 "local_decay_exponents":{k:str(v) for k,v in decays.items()},
 "local_factor_error_constant":336,
 "prime_norm_cutoff":10**10,
 "product_deviation_upper_bound":str(2*local_sum),
 "certificate_polynomial_residual":"0",
 "perturbation_identity_residual":"0",
 "local_rational_identity_residuals":["0","0"],
 "reflected_energy_identity_residuals":["0","0","0","0"],
 "reflected_energy_branch_slack_constants":[str(branch1),str(branch2)],
 "reflected_energy_slack_constant":11,
 "norm_lift_inert_polynomial_residual":"0",
 "large_bin_endpoint_identity_residual":"0",
 "large_bin_uniform_model_margin":str(large_margin),
 "capacity_difference_identity_residual":"0",
 "selected_principal_identity_residual":"0",
 "selected_principal_decay_exponents":{k:str(v) for k,v in selected_decays.items()},
 "selected_principal_local_error_constant":2048,
 "principal_contour_identity_residuals":["0","0","0"],
 "conditional_low_exponent_at_candidate":str(R(749999,4000000)),
 "ideal_moderate_row_extension":str(extension),
 "ideal_extended_endpoint_margin":str(new_margin-2*extension),
 "continuous_range_proof":"See answer.tex and research_notes.tex; no grid sampling is used as proof.",
 "sympy_version":S.__version__
}
(BASE/"audit_certificates.json").write_text(json.dumps(output,indent=2)+"\n")
print(json.dumps(output,indent=2))

\end{verbatim}

\section{Audited recursive moment bridge and exact source locations}
The bounded worker handoff inspected the proofs at the pinned source,
not only their theorem statements.  The independent SymPy transcription
\texttt{audit\_moment\_bridge.py} reruns all 32 named algebraic checks
and all eight local residue states from the worker's Sage certificate;
the named checks and state arrays agree exactly.  This verifies algebra,
not an analytic theorem.  Important locations in the raw 16,677-line TeX:
\begin{verbatim}
14.3 general reflected energy: 7747--7804, PDF 97--98
17.1 marked statement: 9250--9270, PDF 115
17.2 canonical statement/proof: 9296--9340; 9789--11599
17.3--17.4 finite propagation: 9366--9550
(17.43) inverse-moving-characters: 10887--10911
(17.48),(17.51)--(17.52) child invariants/drop: 11044--11110
(17.60),(17.64) fibre and normalization: 11279--11449
(17.85)--(17.86) initialization: 12194--12289
17.6 amplification: 12362--12469, PDF 151--152
18.1 plain statement/proof: 12531--12579; 12604--14973
(18.32) child width: 13765--13829
(18.39)--(18.40) slot removal and strict-edge errors: 14182--14309
18.2--18.3 centered invariant/cancellation: 13953--13995;14545--14680
(18.52) mesh/order closure and proof: 14809--14973
19.2 row counts and capacity comparison: 15185--15446
\end{verbatim}

The marked dependency is \(14.3\Rightarrow17.2\Rightarrow17.1
\Rightarrow17.6\); plain 18.1 does not use 17.1.
The canonical column class is
\[
 \bar\alpha(n)\gamma_2(n)\nu(n)\rho(n)
       \chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4\mathfrak d(n),
\]
where \(f\) is averaged squarefree, \(\rho\) is fixed outside both
averages, and \(\mathfrak d\) has product-form independent marks.
There is no arbitrary bounded residual column coefficient.

In the first transform (17.25), the common factor is
\(\chi_n(J)^4 1_{(n,\operatorname{rad}q_0)=1}\).
All exponent-zero factors remain punctures.  Weighted Cauchy is
used before removing the old label through \(f^2E\mid(y)\);
the resulting bound is a divisor-fibre bound, not another free label
count.  In the second transform,
\[
 k'=d_kd_2k'',\quad f'=JCd_2v',\qquad
 \rho'(n)=\rho(n)1_{(n,\operatorname{rad}(q_0t'r_g))=1}.
\]
Original zeros force \(J,C,d_2,v'\) squarefree and pairwise coprime
before positivity.  Only the source-supported triple
\((q_0,t',r_g)\) is fixed for the child; a counting ball may not
replace its supported projection.  The reconstruction multiplicity
is bounded by \(d(f')^{9+4K}\) times a divisor weight of this triple,
independently of the row.  Thus the four constituents of \(f'\)
are not counted again.
The exact child ledgers are
\[
 F_c=F-D_c-2\ell+j,\quad M_c=M-2D_c-2\ell+j,\quad
 \kappa_i+\lambda_c+C_c+2F_c=F.
\]
The margins lose at most \(5\eta,7\eta\), row length drops by
\(2(V+\ell)-8\eta\ge d\), and energy costs \(40\eta+\tau+\pi\).
Initialization preserves the same type of puncture and has margin
losses \(9\eta+\tau_{\rm init}\), \(22\eta+3\tau_{\rm init}\)
and energy loss \(11\eta+\pi_{\rm init}+\epsilon_c\).

The sixth-power amplification identity
\[
 M_u(D)=\sum_{d\mid\operatorname{rad}a}
 \mu(d)\psi_u(d)q_d^{-1/2}M_{ua^6}(D/q_d)
\]
holds even when \(a,u\) share primes.  The map \((u,a)\mapsto ua^6\)
is injective on sixth-power-free \(u\).  The scale supremum precedes
the averaging.  A fixed small preliminary amplification parameter
gives \(e(r)=\max(1,(1+5r)/6)\), uniformly at \(r=1\) and on
bounded \(r\)-ranges, without shrinking a strict marked-moment margin.

For the plain proof, both transforms retain complete common supports,
including overlaps between slots and plain variables.  Full
M\"obius identities precede any off-coprime estimate.  Whole-product
Fourier separation preserves a common character, common mask and
norm power inside each centered rectangle.  The two child inducing
characters differ by an element of \(\Theta\).
Its width is \(M'=M+J-g-g_2+t_2\le M-\sigma\).
Zero-slot bands are completed first with unrestricted plain lengths;
then removing every live slot does not create a same-band cycle.
Exceptional children are counted in their prescribed sixth-power
form, not omitted.  A multiplicity-one nonunit prime forces valuation
four in the child, which is used in the local support ledger.

For centered cancellation, the masked fixed-lattice main coefficient
is independent of both scale and height, so equal-product-scale
main terms cancel exactly.  Formal subunit scales are not clipped.
The identity
\[
 t_- -\widetilde r_1-\widetilde r_2-(r-\widetilde r_1)_+
 =(t_- -\widetilde r_2)-\max(r,\widetilde r_1)\le\omega_2-r
\]
pays for the divisor count.  Combining it with
\(F_1+F_2\ge2v/3-O(\sigma+\xi)\) leaves the terminal loss
\((A-M)_+\), not a loss repeated per slot.

All support constants for a fixed \(N\)-slot system are first
aggregated into \(\theta_N=H_N/\log Z\).
Greedy removal has one overshoot,
\(\kappa d_z\le F_{\rm act}/6+\eta\), and the strict-edge error
is \(\delta_{\rm fr,2}+\delta_{\rm fr,1}/6+\eta+7\theta_N/3\).
Thus the mesh is chosen before \(N\); constants, smooth orders,
divisor shares and the eventual threshold may depend on fixed \(N\).
At \(\kappa=1\) the prime bound is absolute counting; at
\(\kappa=3/4\), \(6\kappa-1=7/2\) is positive, and no positive
\(\kappa-3/4\) is used.  Euler derivatives of common profiles
\(K(R e^{L(x)})\) cost no \(Z^{J\xi}\); internal measures retain
their weighted integrals.  Increasing a later external tail order
does not change those internal height degrees.

\section{Conditional actual counts, including the floor distinction}
Let \(B\in(\theta_\lambda,1]\),
\(\Delta_+=(B-7/8)_+\), and \(\kappa=3/4+2\Delta_+\).
The plain positive-slot condition \(B\le(1+\kappa)/2\) is automatic
for \(\kappa<1\); at one use its unconditional prime-counting
version.  The detector and its saturated witness proof have range
\(a\le1\), so no preliminary \(11/12\) theorem is required.
For actual nonfloor bins \(\delta=2a-1>1/50\), its witness
satisfies
\[
 r\le t+o(1),\quad r+m\ge t-o(1),\quad
 r\ge t-1/2-O(\epsilon),\quad m\le1/2+O(\epsilon).
\]
The ideal inverse/plain spikes have exponents \(\delta r,\delta m\).

Fix physical Mellin parameters, an error subset, and an amplitude
vector pointwise.  Assign error slots amplitude zero.  The lower
spike is used only on selected positive main amplitudes.
Relative to the negative witness presentation
\(\nu\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)}\), the physical coefficient is
\(\bar\nu(p)1_{p\in1_T}\).  Conjugating the whole plain product
gives \(\nu(p)1_{p\in1_T}\).  These are fixed finite combinations
of members of \(\Theta\); witness and physical heights may differ
but Sobolev does not change these coefficients or their masks.

For \(0<\delta\le5/6\), the two selected capacities are
\(z_M=(1-r)/2\) and \(z_P=(1-2m)/(6\kappa)\).
With \(x=q/\delta\) and \(D=3-17x/9\), the baseline crossing is
\[
 r_*=\frac{(2-8x/9)t-5x/9}{D},\qquad
 r_*\ge23/37,\quad 1/3\le t-r_*\le1/2.
\]
Inverse selection on \(r\ge r_*\), decremented by \(\nu_0\),
has margins \(2\nu_0\) and \(9/37\), and capacity at most \(7/37\).
Plain capacity is at most \(2/27+O(\epsilon)\).
The capacity increment on the enlarged \(\kappa\)-range is
\[
 \frac{24q(1-2m)\Delta_+}{(9/2)(9/2+12\Delta_+)}
 \le\frac{40}{243}\Delta_++O(\epsilon)
 \le\Delta_+/4+O(\epsilon).
\]
Its constants are uniform on \(\Delta_+\in[0,1/8]\).
At zero use the actual moment with \(\kappa=3/4\), not a limit.

The short/long comparison functions are
\[
 1-\delta+\frac{\delta P}{D}(3/2-t),\qquad
 1-\delta+(5/6-\delta)(t-1).
\]
Their intersection
\(t=1+\delta P/(2\{(5/6-\delta)D+\delta P\})\)
gives \(R_*\).  If an inverse capacity is at most \(\nu_0\),
the no-slot count is \(1-\delta+2\delta\nu_0\); if a plain
capacity is at most \(\nu_0\), it is \(1-\delta+6\delta\nu_0\).
These neighborhoods have fixed constants.
For \(5/6\le\delta\le1\), use \(t=3/2\) and the amplification
identity to get \(R\le1-\delta+\epsilon\).
For \(t=1\), no slots, the two affine counts cross at \(r=2/3\),
giving \(1-2\delta/3+\epsilon\) only for nonfloor bins.

At the floor \(\delta_0=1/50\), retain \(R=1\).
There need not be a witnessing zero there (source Proposition 8.3
requires \(a>51/100\)).  The false floor extension would give
\(R=74/75\).  Under the same moment inputs, after choosing a
sufficiently small height power the nonfloor counts have total
\(O(U^{1-c})\) for some \(c>0\), whereas sixth-power-free physical
elements in a dyad have positive density.  The latter follows
by inclusion--exclusion over sixth-power ideal divisors and the
planar ideal count.  Thus the floor has \(\gg U\) rows under
these inputs and cannot satisfy \(74/75+\epsilon\) for arbitrary
small \(\epsilon\).  This obstruction is already avoided by the
manuscript's explicit floor case.

\section{Nonprincipal analytic audit and contour extension}
The relevant raw sections are:
\begin{verbatim}
4.8--4.10 strip/logarithmic/deleted-factor estimates: 1415--1646
7.1 full local identity and all six valuations: 3847--4189
8.1 buffered bins: 4281--4383
8.2 and 8.3 dyadic bounds and saturated detector: 4385--4685
10.2 external integrated and trace tails: 5666--5801
10.3 and 10.4 finite-bin contours and exponent: 5803--6165
10.6 outer dyadic norms: 6335--6463
16.1 quotient-free selected correction: 8651--8798
16.1 dynamic local errors: 8800--9049
16.2 absolute tuple bounds: 9095--9196
\end{verbatim}
All have been read in the authenticated source for the enlarged
application.  The following checks explain which printed
restrictions can be removed and which cannot.

Source logarithmic control is stronger than a bare polynomial
reciprocal bound.  On a zero-free disk centered at \(2+it\),
Borel--Carath\'eodory bounds a fixed logarithm by \(O_e(\log\mathcal C)\).
It is bounded uniformly on the radius \(49/100\) Euler disk.
Three-circles on radii \(R_4,R_6\) then gives
\(O_e((\log\mathcal C)^\vartheta)\), with \(\vartheta<1\)
uniformly for \(a\in[1/2,1]\).  Exponentiating gives
\(\mathcal C^\epsilon\) for any requested \(\epsilon\).
The principal regularizer is \((s-1)\zeta_F(s)/(s+1)\).
Finite deleted factors are subpower on positive strips and cost
at most the negative real part on negative strips.  There is no
implicit assumption of a uniform zero-free strip here: the disks
are first made zero-free by the bins or the definition of \(B\).

The exact high identity (16.7) follows from base identity (7.5),
not (7.3).  The finite operation at one selected prime has factor
\(Q^{z-1}G_p\) after extracting exactly the same scalar local
factor as at an unselected prime.  Disjoint windows prohibit
multiple use of one prime.  The tuple correction is continued
without any \(P_p\) or \(H_p\) division, including at their zeros.
It has a fixed-power, all-height majorant on the first Euler region
\(\mathcal D_1(e_0)\); this is independent of the geometry.

For a fixed dynamic bin one may extend source Lemmas 10.3--10.4
below \(7/8\) directly.  Move \(z:2\to17/50\), \(s:3\to B+20e\)
with \(w=3\); move \(w:3\to1-a-6e\); truncate the three original
height axes; then move the retained \(s\) segment to \(a+16e\).
The first \(w\)-move has \(\Re(s+w)\ge1+(B-a)+14e\);
the last rectangle has \(\Re(s+w)\ge1+10e\).
Also \(a\le B\le1\), so \(\Re w\ge-6e>-1/100\).
No second Euler region is needed.  The numerator is nonprincipal,
the scalar zeta argument stays \(6z>1\), and the buffered reciprocal
has no pole.  This proof does not use the printed parameter
restriction \(\sigma_0\ge7/8\).

All discarded pieces and joins satisfy \(O_{\eta,N}(Z^{K_\eta}T^{-N})\),
with \(K_\eta\) fixed before \(N\).  On an \(s\)-join, the only
buffered argument is \(s\) itself, so extending the \(w,z\) axes
uses global estimates only.  The independent height coordinates
\((\Im(s+z),\Im z,\Im w)\) make the test rapidly decreasing in all
directions.  The trace estimate is proved by direct integration on
a fixed-height face, not inferred from a mere integrated tail.

The dynamic error statement source Proposition 16.1 already has
the full range \(51/100\le a\le1\); no new parameter extension
is required there.  On its line put \(w_r=1-a-6e\),
\(A_*=1/2-w_r\).  A good prime of valuation \(j\in\{1,\ldots,5\}\)
in a physical row has primitive conductor exponent exactly one.
For all distinct strict ramified labels simultaneously,
\[
 q_{\mathfrak f_u}\ll_S q_u\prod_{p\in J_0}q_p^{-(j_p-1)}.
\]
A strict label occurs only for \(j_p\ge2\); its exponent becomes
\(-w_r-(j_p-1)A_*\le-1/2\).
Thus the numerator is used once for the entire error subset.
At unramified selected labels, the four exponents are
\[
 -a-4e,\quad-51/25+12e,\quad49/25-5a-68e,\quad
 a-51/25+12e,
\]
all at most \(-51/100\).
The non-tail ramified boundary exponents after extracting \(Q^z\)
are
\[
 a-2+6e,\quad1/2-2a-32e,\quad
 -a-26e,\quad1-3a-48e,\quad
 1/2-2a-42e,\quad2-5a-80e,
\]
all below \(-1/2\).
The script certifies these affine bounds on the whole continuous
rectangle by exact vertex checking after checking affinity.
This is not numerical sampling.  The statement's lower bound
\(|H_p|\ge1/2\) on this dynamic region is secured by an enlarged
fixed cutoff depending on \(e\); it is not asserted globally.

For the enlarged small-row line \(s_r=B+e\), \(w_r=1/2\),
\(z_r=17/50\), \(\mathcal D_1(7/20)\) suffices.
It is important not to reuse the literal \(\mathcal D_1(3/8)\)
line from the source when \(B<7/8\).
The off-row selected error has exponents
\(-s_r,-6z_r,4-5s_r-6z_r,1/2-6z_r\), all negative at
\(s_r\ge43/50\).
The ramified boundary exponents after multiplication by \(Q^{s_r}\)
are
\[
 -1/2,\quad3/2-2s_r,\quad
 (3/2-2s_r,2-3s_r),\quad2-3s_r,\quad3-5s_r.
\]
At \(s_r=43/50\) they are respectively
\(-1/2,-11/50,(-11/50,-29/50),-29/50,-13/10\).
The strict term has exponent \(1/2\), so \(G_p\ll Q^{1/2}\)
on a row prime and \(G_p=O(1)\) elsewhere.  Divisor counting
then gives the positive tuple bound \(U^\epsilon Z^{\ell z_r}\)
without dividing by \(H_p\).
The numerator's crude strip bound is \(U^{3/5+\epsilon}\);
the reciprocal has subpower conductor growth.
This proves the small-row estimate at the new boundary, including
a principal denominator on a nonidentity unit row.

\section{Unified exact high margins and height order}
The conditional actual counts now instantiate the affine model.
Put \(\zeta=10^{-6}\), \(d_{\min}=1/100\).
For \(d\in[1/2,h+\zeta]\) the supply exceeds \(1/5\),
since \(5\ell-h-\zeta=1/48+7\lambda/2-\zeta>0\).
For \(\delta\le5/6\) use \(R_*+\Delta_+/4\) above the floor;
at the floor use \(R=1\).
For \(d\le1/2,\delta\le5/6\), the common count
\(76/75-2\delta/3\) also covers the floor.
For all \(d\le h+\zeta\) and \(\delta\ge5/6\), use \(1-\delta\).

With \(q\le\delta/2\), the error-free endpoint values are
\[
\begin{gathered}
 E_\lambda(h;1/50,1/100,1)=-7/1200+32\lambda/25,\\
 E_\lambda(1/2;\delta,\delta/2,76/75-2\delta/3)
 =-529/2400+25\delta/96+\lambda(13/50+3\delta/4),\\
 E_\lambda(h;\delta,\delta/2,1-\delta)
 =-1/48-\delta/16+5\lambda/4.
\end{gathered}
\]
The middle expression is at most
\(-49/14400+177\lambda/200\).
Every slope is positive and less than two; the adaptive minimum
comes from the completed-square certificate in the manuscript.
Its extended margin is
\[
 \frac{59873}{550800000}-\frac2{10^6}
 =\frac{293857}{2754000000}>\frac1{10000}.
\]
For the adaptive range compare directly to \(C(B)\):
the extra \(h\Delta_+/4\) is absorbed by \(B-\theta_\lambda\).
This remains valid when \(B<7/8\), because then \(\Delta_+=0\)
and \(B>\theta_\lambda\); no clipping cost is paid.

The small-row bound has exponent
\(C(B)-63/800+(51/100)\lambda+O(e+\epsilon)\).
For the unbounded large rows choose the fixed
\(z_\infty=4000000\).  At \(\zeta=10^{-6}\) the maximum of
\[
 B_0+(h+\zeta)(1+\epsilon)-\zeta z_\infty+\epsilon
\]
over \(0\le\lambda,\epsilon\le10^{-6}\) is exactly
\(-612498574999/400000000000<-1\).
This is an exact multi-affine-box bound, not an assertion from a grid.
Large real contours can make test constants enormous; they remain
fixed before \(Z\) and the external decay order.

Choose moment losses, capacity decrement and mesh before the
target.  Choose even \(K\), \(\ell_i=\ell/K\), and disjoint nonzero
nonnegative bumps in the intervals
\((1+(2i-1)/(2K+1),1+2i/(2K+1))\).
Then \(\mu=(43/50)\ell/K>0\) is common.
Choose \(e\) and amplitude/normalizer losses after these slots so
that the nonprincipal saving remains at least \(1/40000\) and
half each principal saving remains.  The low loss can be
\((B-\theta)/2\), independently of the high margin.
There is no condition comparing \(\lambda/4\) to \(\mu\).

Only now fix a target and its arithmetic data, with a common
excluded set in the low probe, high identity, normalizer and
signal.  Let \(A_\eta,K_\eta\) be the finite retained height and
discarded scale degrees, fixed before the external order.
Allocate all added Mellin frequencies once, by
\(c_k=1/[2n(1+\max_j|a_{jk}|)]\).
Choose \(\tau>0\) meeting the detector ceiling and
\(\tau A_\eta<m/4\); then choose \(N\) with
\(K_\eta-N\tau<C(B)-m/2\).
The functions themselves contain neither \(\tau\) nor \(N\).
This produces the common high saving \(m/2\) and closes the Mellin
contradiction under \(\mathsf A\).  The conclusion is strict:
zeros on \(\Re s=\theta\) are not excluded.

\section{Full reflection: source comparison requirements}
The specialized reflection issue is not just the elementary
gamma-contour shift.  Source Proposition 5.1 imports the automorphy and
the three cusp expansions \(F_1,F_{19},F_{10}\) from
Dunn--Radziwill v3, Section 5 and Appendix A.
It constructs translated theta values using \emph{all} finite
Fourier frequencies.  The standard source unit-supported twisted
corollary cannot be substituted for this construction.

Some direct checks that support its route: the inverse hyperbolic
coordinate at the cusp \(a/c\) is
\[
 g^{-1}(z+a/c,v)=
 \left(-\delta'/c-\bar z/[c^2(v^2+|z|^2)],
             v/[q_c(v^2+|z|^2)]\right).
\]
At \(z=0\), its \(\bar z\)-derivative is \(-(cv)^{-2}\) in the
first coordinate, zero in its conjugate and vertical coordinates.
Thus horizontal differentiation kills every cusp constant term.
The direct angular factor \(\bar\alpha(nb^3)\) is essential to
the claimed entire continuation of the completed product;
an untwisted cubic theta series with a possible main term is not
a substitute.
The direct/reflected Mellin ratio gives
\[
 R(t)=\prod_{\pm}
 \frac{\Gamma(1+t\pm1/6)}{\Gamma(1-t\pm1/6)},\qquad
 -\frac{i}{81}\bar\alpha(c)^2q_\mu^{-1/2}
 \left(\frac{(2\pi)^4q_\mu X}{27q_c^2}\right)^{-t}.
\]
The first negative numerator pole is \(-5/6\).
The kernel's \(-1/4\) line and all Euler-derivative estimates
follow from the exact full-support reflected identity proved in
Appendix B of the manuscript.

The further requirements for the energy application are stronger
than the existence of a formula for one fixed row.  The same
three cusp functions must be used when a zero-exponent prime is
inactive or absent; the active radical fixes the cusp data.
Separate row and slot sectors modulo \(M_{\rm ref}^2\) must remove
every row--slot phase without absorbing a moving good prime into
the fixed modulus.  Residual row exponents are \(j=1\), active
mark exponents \(j=0\); their pair phase is one.
All other base primes, especially \(j=4\), are frozen before the
inner norm.  A fixed fourth-power label may grow with \(Z\),
so constants in the general energy estimate may not depend on
that label as if it were a fixed arithmetic datum.
These full-support and uniformity points are maintained in the
full-frequency derivation recorded below and in Appendix B of the
manuscript.


\paragraph{Literature and standard continuation receipt.}
The exact-title and candidate-constant searches on 8 October 2026
returned release descriptions and the same heuristic geometry reports;
no smaller proved half-plane was obtained from those searches.
No result from a claimed formal build at another revision is used.
The directly checked standard references are DLMF 25.15(i)
(especially 25.15.4) and Poonen's Theorem 5.22(b), Section 5.11,
PDF page 38.  The latter concerns the completed Hecke function;
removal of the archimedean gamma factor introduces no new pole
on the positive half-plane.  Neither reference is being cited for the new descent itself.

\section{Additional exact scripts and certificates}
\texttt{audit\_nonprincipal.py} has 71 named checks; all pass.
It tests the entire affine parameter boxes by proving the expressions
multi-affine before checking the vertices.  Rational identities are
checked by exact factorization.  These do not establish \(\mathsf A\).
The pre-existing \texttt{audit\_checks.py} also runs successfully.
The separate worker Sage script is preserved in the downloadable
audit bundle; the following portable transcription retains all
32 of its named checks and all eight local residue states.
\subsection{Moment-bridge transcription}
\begin{verbatim}
"""Exact checks for the round-2 moment audit; no analytic theorem is certified.

Run: python audit_moment_bridge.py
SymPy transcription of the preserved Sage worker script; all original algebraic checks retained.
All identities use QQ polynomial/fraction fields, not numerical sampling.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib
import json
import sympy as S
QQ = S.Rational
BASE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent

out = {"scope": "Exact algebra and finite residue-state identities only; see the audit report for analytic dependencies."}
checks = []

def ring(names):
    return None, S.symbols(names)

def ident(name, lhs, rhs=0):
    assert S.factor(lhs-rhs) == 0, (name, lhs-rhs)
    checks.append(name)

# Section 17: complete local state table, including the marked zero state.
states = []
for parity in (0, 1):
    for a1 in (0, 1):
        for a2 in (0, 1):
            t = (a1-a2+3*parity) % 6
            e1 = (4*a1 + (t != 0)*(1+t) + parity*(a1+a2)) % 6
            e2 = (4*a2 + (t != 0)*(1-t) + parity*(a1+a2)) % 6
            target = 4 if parity or a1*a2 else 0
            assert e1 == e2 == target
            assert (t != 0)-a1-a2+parity*(a1+a2) == parity-2*(not parity)*a1*a2
            states.append([parity, a1, a2, t, e1, e2])
out["section17_complete_local_states"] = states
checks.append("8 local states in L10262-10272 and actual-norm identity L10333-10334")

R, (m,r,ell,V,A,B,t,g,th,v,j,delt,rad) = ring("m r ell V A B t g th v j delt rad")
F = r+3*ell+V
s = r-A-B-t
Hc = 2*r-2*B-m+4*ell+2*V+delt-j
kap = m-2*r-2*ell-V-delt+A+B-rad/2
lc = Hc-th-(s-g)
Cc = ell+rad/2-j+t+g-th
Dc = ell+A+t+g-th+V
Nc = r-A-B-t-g-v
Vc = B+th+v+j
Fc = Nc+Vc
Mc = 2*(s-g)-Hc+delt+2*th
ident("17.48 F transition", Fc, F-Dc-2*ell+j)
ident("17.48 M transition", Mc, m-2*Dc-2*ell+j)
ident("17.48 first margin", Fc-Mc, F-m+Dc)
ident("17.48 second margin", 4*Fc-3*Mc, 4*F-3*m+2*(A+t+g-th+V)+j)
ident("17.64 prefactor", kap+lc+Cc+2*Fc, F)
ident("17.39 principal ledger", kap+Hc+s+B+t+ell+rad/2, F-B-j)
ident("17.65 perturbation coefficient", QQ(9)/2+18+QQ(11)/2+2*6, 40)

R, (m,r,z,G,P) = ring("m r z G P")
Dp=r+z-2*G
z0=z-G
Fi=Dp-P
Mi=2*Dp-m-2*P
ki=m-2*Dp+P
ident("17 initialization first invariant", Fi-Mi-(P+G)-z0, m-r-2*z+2*G)
ident("17 initialization second invariant", 4*Fi-3*Mi-6*z0, 3*m-2*r-8*z+10*G+2*P)
ident("17 initialization energy", ki+P+2*Fi, m)

# Section 18: identities are checked before max/positive-part inequalities.
R, (m,q,A,c,d,p,Rr,E,K,w,wo,gg,ll,c2,d2,p2,g2,t2,V,f,Bc,Bd,s0) = ring(
    "m q A c d p Rr E K w wo gg ll c2 d2 p2 g2 t2 V f Bc Bd s0")
M=m+q
qt=q+Rr+E
K0=2*A-c-d+Rr+E-m
a0=A-c-w
J=d-c+(K0-K)-2*w+wo
mp=2*a0-K-gg-g2-V
qp=qt+wo+t2+V
Mp=mp+qp
b2=(c2+d2)/2
allow=b2-p2+w+Bc+ll
ident("18.32 child width", Mp, M+J-gg-g2+t2)
ident("unnumbered first aggregate ledger after 18.23",
      m-A-Rr/2-E+p+s0+(A-c+qt+Bc-s0+A-d+qt+Bd-s0)/2,
      M+(Bc+Bd-(c+d-2*p-Rr))/2)
ident("18.31 diagonal deficit",
      K+gg-ll-s0-(a0+qt+wo+w+Bc-s0),
      A-M-d-(K0-K)-wo-Bc+gg-ll)
ledger=K+gg-ll-a0+p2-s0+g2-t2-b2
ident("18.33 child allowance", a0+qt+wo+w+Bc-s0-ledger, Mp+allow)
F1=c/6+5*(d+K0-K)/6+w/3+qt/6+wo+Bc-5*gg/6+ll
F2=2*b2-5*g2/6-p2+t2+V/6+f/6
ident("18.42 exceptional deficit", (mp-f)/6+a0-b2-(Mp+allow), A-5*M/6-F1-F2)
ident("18.38 affine child difference",
      (A-c-w-c2-Mp)-(A-M),
      gg+w-d-c2-(K0-K)-wo+g2-t2)

# Local infinite families in F2: displayed residual polynomials are
# nonnegative on their stated domains. These are symbolic equalities.
R, (i,jj) = ring("i jj")
ident("F2 unit residual", 7*i/6-2*i/3, i/2)
ident("F2 nonunit i>=2 residual", (7*i-5)/6-2*i/3, (3*i-5)/6)
ident("F2 nonunit i=1 equality", QQ(7-5)/6+QQ(1)/3, QQ(2)/3)
ident("F2 equal six-divisible residual", 7*i/6-1-2*i/3, (i-2)/2)
ident("F2 unequal residual", i+jj/6-1-(i+jj)/3, (4*i-jj-6)/6)

# Full-range row envelopes and exact parameter bounds.
R, (delta,x,t,Delta,r,mm,lam) = ring("delta x t Delta r mm lam")
alpha=QQ(5)/6
D=3-17*x/9
P=(2-8*x/9)*(1-x)
Jj=(alpha-delta)*D+delta*P
rs=((2-8*x/9)*t-5*x/9)/D
AI=1-delta*(x+(1-x)*r)
St=1-delta*(4*x/9+(2-8*x/9)*(t-r))
Rshort=1-delta+delta*P/D*(QQ(3)/2-t)
ident("19.7 weighted crossing", ((2-8*x/9)*AI+(1-x)*St)/D, Rshort)
ident("rstar upper endpoint", rs.subs({t:QQ(3)/2}), 1)
ident("rstar lower-endpoint margin", rs.subs({t:1})-QQ(23)/37,
      (1-2*x)*5/(37*D))
ident("plain crossing minimum margin", (t-rs).subs({t:1})-QQ(1)/3,
      5*x/(27*D))
topt=1+delta*P/(2*Jj)
Rstar=1-delta+(alpha-delta)*delta*P/(2*Jj)
ident("optimal adaptive short envelope", Rshort.subs({t:topt}), Rstar)
ident("optimal adaptive long envelope", 1-delta+(alpha-delta)*(topt-1), Rstar)
capdiff=2*(x*delta)*(1-2*mm)*(QQ(2)/9-1/(QQ(9)/2+12*Delta))
ident("19.6 exact capacity change", capdiff,
      24*x*delta*(1-2*mm)*Delta/((QQ(9)/2)*(QQ(9)/2+12*Delta)))
# On delta<=5/6, x<=1/2, mm>=1/3, Delta>=0,
# capdiff <= (40/243) Delta < Delta/4 (at exact lengths).
capratio=QQ(24)*(QQ(5)/12)*(QQ(1)/3)/(QQ(9)/2)**2
assert capratio==QQ(40)/243 < QQ(1)/4
out["capacity_increment_over_Delta_bound_exact_lengths"] = str(capratio)
ident("large-bin amplification excess", (1+5*r)/6-delta*r-(1-delta), (alpha-delta)*(r-1))
ident("unselected t=1 crossing", 1-delta*QQ(2)/3, 1-2*delta/3)
ell=QQ(1)/6+lam
h=QQ(13)/16+3*lam/2
zeta=QQ(1)/10**6
ident("supply inequality numerator", 5*ell-h-zeta, QQ(1)/48+7*lam/2-zeta)
assert QQ(1)/48-zeta>0
out["minimum_supply_at_lambda0_dmax"] = str((QQ(1)/6)/(QQ(13)/16+zeta))
out["marked_second_margin_lower_bound"] = "9/37"
out["floor_unweighted_claimed_exponent_if_misapplied"] = str(1-QQ(2)/3*QQ(1)/50)

pins=[]
for f in json.loads((BASE/'source_manifest.json').read_text()):
    path=BASE/f['filename']
    digest=hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    assert digest==f['expected_sha256']
    pins.append({"file":str(path),"bytes":path.stat().st_size,"sha256":digest})
out["pins_rechecked"] = pins
out["identity_checks"] = checks
out["identity_count"] = len(checks)
out["all_exact_checks_passed"] = True
(BASE/'moment_bridge_certificates.json').write_text(json.dumps(out,indent=2)+'\n')
print(json.dumps({"all_exact_checks_passed":True,"identity_count":len(checks),
                  "certificate":"moment_bridge_certificates.json"},indent=2))
\end{verbatim}
\subsection{Nonprincipal verification script}
\begin{verbatim}
"""Exact checks for the conditional descent in answer.tex.
Run: python audit_nonprincipal.py
All arithmetic is rational or symbolic over QQ. No grid certifies an analytic claim.
The affine box checks use every vertex, which proves their bounds by convexity.
No check establishes Arithmetic Hypothesis A or an unconditional zero-free region.
"""
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
import json
import sympy as S

QQ = S.Rational
BASE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
checks = []
def eq(name, lhs, rhs=0):
    residual = S.factor(lhs-rhs)
    assert residual == 0, (name, residual)
    checks.append(name)
def test(name, statement):
    assert statement, name
    checks.append(name)
def affine_max(expr, coords):
    """Certify multi-affinity first, then maximize exactly on the real box."""
    poly = S.Poly(S.expand(expr), *coords)
    assert all(poly.degree(x) <= 1 for x in coords)
    return max(expr.subs(dict(zip(coords, values)))
               for values in product(*(coords[x] for x in coords)))

la,d,de,x,q,R,t,r,m,Delta,nu,e = S.symbols(
    "lambda d delta x q R t r m Delta nu e", real=True)
alpha=QQ(5,6); z0=QQ(17,50); zeta=QQ(1,10**6)
ell=QQ(1,6)+la
lx=QQ(17,48)-la/2; ly=QQ(23,48)-la/2
h=QQ(13,16)+3*la/2
theta=QQ(7,8)-la/4
a=(1+de)/2
C=lambda s:s-QQ(11,16)
E=lambda dd,delta,amplitude,RR: S.expand(
    (1+delta)/2-theta+h*(z0-QQ(1,6))
    -(1+delta)*ly/2-ell/2+amplitude*ell
    +dd*(RR+delta/2-z0))
eq("C(s) geometry", lx/2-1+h/6, -QQ(11,16))
eq("low boundary", C(theta), lx/2+QQ(1,8)/12)
eq("new boundary", theta.subs(la,QQ(1,10**6)), QQ(3499999,4000000))

# Selection and full-range envelope calculations.
D=3-QQ(17,9)*x
P=(2-QQ(8,9)*x)*(1-x)
J=(alpha-de)*D+de*P
rstar=((2-8*x/9)*t-5*x/9)/D
AI=1-de*(x+(1-x)*r)
St=1-de*(4*x/9+(2-8*x/9)*(t-r))
Rshort=1-de+de*P/D*(QQ(3,2)-t)
tstar=1+de*P/(2*J)
Rs=1-de+(alpha-de)*de*P/(2*J)
eq("crossing equality", AI.subs(r,rstar),St.subs(r,rstar))
eq("crossing weighted value",((2-8*x/9)*AI+(1-x)*St)/D,Rshort)
eq("rstar at t=3/2",rstar.subs(t,QQ(3,2)),1)
eq("rstar lower margin",rstar.subs(t,1)-QQ(23,37),5*(1-2*x)/(37*D))
eq("plain-length lower margin",(t-rstar).subs(t,1)-QQ(1,3),5*x/(27*D))
eq("short optimum",Rshort.subs(t,tstar),Rs)
eq("long optimum",1-de+(alpha-de)*(tstar-1),Rs)
eq("inverse first strict margin",1-r-2*((1-r)/2-nu),2*nu)
eq("inverse second strict margin",3-2*r-8*((1-r)/2-nu),2*r-1+8*nu)
eq("inverse second margin endpoint",2*QQ(23,37)-1,QQ(9,37))
capchange=2*q*(1-2*m)*(QQ(2,9)-1/(QQ(9,2)+12*Delta))
eq("capacity change",capchange,
   24*q*(1-2*m)*Delta/(QQ(9,2)*(QQ(9,2)+12*Delta)))
capbound=24*QQ(5,12)*QQ(1,3)/QQ(9,2)**2
eq("capacity constant",capbound,QQ(40,243))
test("capacity < Delta/4 coefficient",capbound<QQ(1,4))
eq("large-bin amplification excess",(1+5*r)/6-de*r-(1-de),
   (alpha-de)*(r-1))
eq("unselected t=1 crossing",1-de*QQ(2,3),1-2*de*(1-QQ(2,3)))

# Moderate contours and rational continuous-domain certificate.
raw = lx*(QQ(1,2)-z0)+(a+16*e)+z0-1+ly*(-a-6*e)
raw += -d*z0+d*(R+de/2)+ell*(z0-QQ(1,2))+q*ell
eq("central high exponent",raw-C(theta),E(d,de,q,R)+(16-6*ly)*e)
floor=E(h,QQ(1,50),QQ(1,100),1)
eq("floor endpoint",floor,-QQ(7,1200)+QQ(32,25)*la)
inter=E(QQ(1,2),de,de/2,QQ(76,75)-2*de/3)
eq("intermediate endpoint",inter,
   -QQ(529,2400)+QQ(25,96)*de+la*(QQ(13,50)+3*de/4))
eq("intermediate d slope",QQ(76,75)-2*de/3+de/2-z0,
   QQ(101,150)-de/6)
big=E(h,de,de/2,1-de)
eq("large-bin endpoint",big,-QQ(1,48)-de/16+5*la/4)
eq("large-bin d slope",1-de+de/2-z0,QQ(33,50)-de/2)
E0=E(QQ(13,16),de,x*de,Rs).subs(la,0)
eq("unperturbed endpoint form",E0,
   -QQ(1,48)+2*de/3+x*de/6-QQ(13,16)*(1-Rs))
eq("adaptive perturbation",E(h,de,x*de,Rs)-E0,
   la*(-QQ(1,4)+(1+x)*de+3*Rs/2))
y=S.symbols("y", real=True)
v=51+41*y
cert=(3+5*y)*((4*v*de-79)**2+49)
cert+=4*y*(4*v*de*((1+3*y)*(15+32*y)*de+9-13*y)+265+3485*y)
eq("continuous endpoint polynomial",10368*v*(J*(-E0)).subs(x,QQ(1,2)-y),cert)
eq("denominator first-endpoint minimum",alpha*D.subs(x,QQ(1,2)),QQ(185,108))
eq("denominator delta=alpha minimum",alpha*P.subs(x,QQ(1,2)),QQ(35,54))
margin=QQ(49,440640)-QQ(5,2*10**6)
eq("adaptive margin",margin,QQ(59873,550800000))
extended=margin-2*zeta
eq("extended adaptive margin",extended,QQ(293857,2754000000))
test("extended margin > 1/10000",extended>QQ(1,10000))
test("floor extended margin",floor.subs(la,QQ(1,10**6))+2*zeta < -QQ(1,10000))
test("intermediate margin",inter.subs({la:QQ(1,10**6),de:alpha}) < -QQ(1,10000))
test("large-bin extended margin",big.subs({la:QQ(1,10**6),de:alpha})+2*zeta < -QQ(1,10000))
test("supply > 1/5", (5*ell-h-zeta).subs(la,0)>0)
eq("supply numerator",5*ell-h-zeta,QQ(1,48)+7*la/2-zeta)
test("supply exceeds largest inverse capacity",QQ(1,5)>QQ(7,37))
test("adaptive maximum slope < 2", QQ(33,50)+QQ(1,8)/4<2)
test("large-bin minimum slope positive", QQ(33,50)-QQ(1,2)>0)
eq("B>=7/8 comparison",h*Delta/4-(Delta+la/4),
   -(1-h/4)*Delta-la/4)

# All-height analytic-contour exponents; these do not verify the L-function estimates.
eq("small uncounted real exponent",h*(z0-QQ(1,6))-ly/2,
   -QQ(79,800)+QQ(51,100)*la)
eq("small-row power",1+QQ(3,5)-z0,QQ(63,50))
eq("small counted bound",h*(z0-QQ(1,6))-ly/2+QQ(2,100),
   -QQ(63,800)+QQ(51,100)*la)
test("small line D1(7/20)",theta.subs(la,QQ(1,10**6))+QQ(1,2)>1+QQ(7,20))
B0=lx/2+1+ly
eps=S.symbols("eps", real=True)
large=B0+(h+zeta)*(1+eps)-zeta*4000000+eps
large_max=affine_max(large,{la:(0,QQ(1,10**6)),eps:(0,QQ(1,10**6))})
test("unbounded dyadic tail < -1",large_max < -1)

# Selected local factors on the enlarged small-row line.
smin=QQ(43,50)
small_off=[-smin,-6*z0,4-5*smin-6*z0,QQ(1,2)-6*z0]
small_ram=[-QQ(1,2),QQ(3,2)-2*smin,2-3*smin,3-5*smin]
test("small unramified errors bounded",max(small_off)<0)
test("small ramified boundary exponent <= 1/2",max(small_ram)<=QQ(1,2))
test("small R ratio uniform",4-6*smin-6*z0 <= -QQ(16,5))
test("small V ratio uniform",-6*z0 == -QQ(51,25))
large_ram=[-2,QQ(3,2)-4,2-4-2,2-6,QQ(5,2)-6-2,3-10]
test("large ramified boundary exponents negative",max(large_ram)<0)

# Full dynamic range a in [51/100,1], e in [0,10^-3]:
# all exponents are affine, so endpoint checking is a proof on the box.
aa,ee=S.symbols("a e",real=True)
box={aa:(QQ(51,100),1),ee:(0,QQ(1,1000))}
dynamic_off=[-aa-4*ee,-QQ(51,25)+12*ee,
             QQ(49,25)-5*aa-68*ee,aa-QQ(51,25)+12*ee]
dynamic_ram=[aa-2+6*ee,QQ(1,2)-2*aa-32*ee,
             -aa-26*ee,1-3*aa-48*ee,QQ(1,2)-2*aa-42*ee,2-5*aa-80*ee]
for i,ex in enumerate(dynamic_off):
    test("dynamic off-u error %d"%i,affine_max(ex,box)<=-QQ(51,100))
for i,ex in enumerate(dynamic_ram):
    test("dynamic boundary normalized exponent %d"%i,affine_max(ex,box)<-QQ(1,2))
wr=1-aa-6*ee
Astar=QQ(1,2)-wr
eq("strict simultaneous conductor saving at j=2",-wr-Astar,-QQ(1,2))
eq("retained D1 gap",(aa+16*ee)+wr-1,10*ee)
eq("global-to-reflected D1 gap",(S.Symbol('B')+20*ee)+wr-1,
   S.Symbol('B')-aa+14*ee)
# Ramified H_p defect normalized by Q^{-10e}.
xr=aa+16*ee
defects=[1-xr-wr,-xr-wr,QQ(3,2)-3*xr,
         2-3*xr-wr,2-4*xr,QQ(5,2)-4*xr-wr,3-6*xr]
for i,ex in enumerate(defects):
    test("dynamic ramified H defect %d"%i,affine_max(ex+10*ee,box)<=0)

out={
 "primary_target_proved":False,
 "zeta_only_unconditional_improvement_proved":False,
 "scope":"Exact algebra in a conditional descent; Arithmetic Hypothesis A is not proved by computation.",
 "number_of_exact_checks":len(checks),
 "all_exact_checks_passed":True,
 "checks":checks,

 "candidate_improvement":str(QQ(1,4000000)),
 "ideal_moderate_margin":str(margin),
 "extended_moderate_margin":str(extended),
 "capacity_coefficient_upper_bound":str(capbound),
 "large_tail_maximum_exponent":str(large_max),
 "small_line_unramified_error_exponents":[str(v) for v in small_off],
 "small_line_ramified_boundary_exponents":[str(v) for v in small_ram],
 "sympy_version":S.__version__
}
(BASE/'nonprincipal_certificates.json').write_text(json.dumps(out,indent=2)+"\n")
print(json.dumps({"checks":len(checks),"all_exact_checks_passed":True,
                  "large_tail_maximum_exponent":str(large_max)},indent=2))

\section{Full-frequency theta reflection: proof interface}

Let \(\lambda_3=1+2\omega\), \(e(z)=\check e(z/\lambda_3)\), and
\(\alpha(z)=z/|z|\).  The only theta imports are Dunn--Radziwill
arXiv v3 (14 May 2024), (5.1), (5.4), (5.7)--(5.8), (5.9),
(5.12)--(5.16), and Appendix A rows 1, 10, 19.
Their GRH-conditional prime conclusions and their restricted
unit-Fourier-support twisted reflection are not used.

The three conjugated cusp coefficients are
\[
 \overline{\tau(-\mu)},\quad
 \omega\overline{\tau_1(-\omega^2\mu)}\check e(\mu),\quad
 \omega^2\overline{\tau_2(-\omega\mu)}\check e(\mu).
\]
The sign in \(\overline{d_j(-\mu)}\) is indispensable.
All have support
\(\mu=u\lambda_3^k n b^3\), \(k\ge-4\), \(n\) squarefree,
\(n,b\) primary, with no \((n,b)=1\) restriction and no target
\(S\)-mask.  Their bound is \(27\,3^{k/6}|b|\).
The ordinary cubic Gauss symbol at the excluded primary prime
\(-2\) has norm one after normalization and must be retained.
The infinity mask \(\lambda_3^3\mu\equiv1\pmod3\) selects
\(\mu=\lambda_3^{-3}nb^3\), coefficient
\(3^{5/2}|b|\widetilde g_3(n)\).
Only after the whole-index \(S\)-mask is imposed do we substitute
\(\widetilde g_3(n)=\chi_n(\lambda_3)^{-2}\gamma_2(n)\).

For fixed periodic \(\phi\) and modulus \(L\), every \(h_0\bmod L\)
is present, including nonunits.  The local transforms are
\[
 C_j(h)=
 \begin{cases}
 q_p^{-1/2}\tau_j^-\chi_p(h)^{-j},&j\ne0,h\ne0,\\
 0,&j\ne0,h=0,\\
 -q_p^{-1},&j=0,h\ne0,\\
 1-q_p^{-1},&j=0,h=0 .
 \end{cases}
\]
An active prime has \(h_p\ne0\).  Reducing
\(\lambda_3^2h_0/L=a_F/c_F\) gives
\[
 c=c_Fr,\quad
 a=a_Fr+\lambda_3^2c_F\sum_{p\mid r}(r/p)h_p,\quad
 r=\prod_{\rm active}p .
\]
Local reduction proves \((a,c)=1\) even for nonunit \(h_0\).
Normalize \(a_F\) primary at \(\lambda_3\mid c_F\), otherwise
\(c_F\) primary.  Put \(M=\lambda_3^{12}L^4\).
Lifting the \(h_p\)'s to zero modulo \(M^2\) and fixing
\(r\bmod M^2\) makes \(a\bmod Mc_F\) fixed.
Choose \(\delta'\) inverse to \(a\) on the prime-power parts of
\(Mc_F\) at \(c_F\), zero modulo \(M\) elsewhere on fixed primes,
and inverse to \(a\) at every active prime.
Then \(b=(a\delta'-1)/c\) is integral and all fixed residues
of the determinant-one cusp matrix are determined.

The three multiplier factors are:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 v_{\lambda_3}(c)&\kappa_F\\ \hline
 \ge2&(c_F/a)_3\\
 1&(-u/A)_3((c_F/u)/a)_3,\quad
                   u=\pm\lambda_3,\ A=a-ub\\
 0&(a/c_F)_3 .
 \end{array}
\]
The remaining multiplier is \((a/r)_3\).
In the middle case \(a(c-u\delta')=cA-u\);
in the last case \(-bc=1-a\delta'\equiv1\pmod9\) proves
\((\delta'/(-bc))_3=1\).  These equalities establish the fixed
sector dependence; it is not merely a norm bound for multipliers.

For \(x=\lambda_3^4\mu\), let
\(D_F=\lambda_3^3c_F,\ \sigma_p=\lambda_3^2c/p,\ 
\epsilon_p=-[(\lambda_3^3c/p)\sigma_p]^{-1}\).
Additive CRT gives
\[
 \check e(-\delta'\mu/c)=
 e(-\delta'_F r^{-1}x/D_F)
         \prod_{p\mid r}e(\epsilon_ph_p^{-1}x/p).
\]
The fixed phase is fixed by the same sector.  Substitution
\(y=h_p^{-1}\) in the active frequency sum gives exponent
\(j_p+2\), with the \(j_p=4\) Ramanujan branch and the
separate active \(j_p=0\) branch.  Thus inactive and absent
primes have precisely the same other branch data.  Whole-index
marks by \(1-\chi_p^0\) give scalar \(q_p^{-1}\) on an
inactive mark and the negative active-zero scalar on an active
mark.

The direct Mellin scalar is
\[
 \frac{3^{5/2}}4(27/(2\pi)^2)^s
                      \Gamma(s+1/3)\Gamma(s+2/3).
\]
The reflected scalar is
\[
 -\frac i4(2\pi)^{2s-2}\bar\alpha(c)^2q_c^{1-2s}
 \bar\kappa(G)\Gamma(4/3-s)\Gamma(5/3-s).
\]
The coordinate derivative at the reflected cusp is
\(-1/(c^2v^2)\); all other \(\bar z\)-derivatives vanish at zero,
so every cusp constant is removed.
Bessel decay at both ends makes the Mellin transform entire.
A Gaussian-damped maximum principle transfers polynomial outer-line
bounds to every fixed strip.  At \(s=1/2-t\), the ratio is
\(-i\bar\alpha(c)^2q_\mu^{-1/2}R(t)/81\), with kernel scale
\((2\pi)^4q_\mu X/(27q_c^2)\).
The kernel lines \(-1/4,0,A\) prove
\[
 |(x\partial_x)^jV^\sharp(x)|
 \ll \min(x^{1/4},(1+x)^{-A})
                       \mathfrak M_{A,\lceil4A\rceil+j+2}(V).
\]
These bounds are uniform in the moving arithmetic labels.
In a joint profile \(W(\mathbf y)V^\sharp(Y\prod y_i^{a_i})\),
Euler derivatives introduce only the fixed \(a_i\)'s, no \(Y^J\).
One common density is formed before actual row/tuple evaluation.

\section{General energy and the terminal label normalization}

After all nonresidual base primes are frozen, the cross-pair phase is
\((q/p)_3^{j_p+j_q+2}\).
It is one for \(j_p=1,j_q=0\), not generally for \(j_q=4\).
Separate \(R,P\) classes modulo \(M_{\rm ref}^2\) make the
cusp data common without a pair-dependent condition.
The hybrid coefficient is \(a(P)\), independent of \(R,n,b\),
and the dual coefficient is \(\beta(n,b)\), independent of \(R,P\).
The exact masks left are \((P,R)=1,(P,b)=1\).

Both masks are removed by full Mobius identities before a positive
norm.  The quadratic reduction writes \(b=gt^2,c=(n,g),n=cm,g=ch\),
and uses
\(\chi_k(nb)^3=\chi_k(mh)^3 1_{(k,ct)=1}\).
It allows \((g,t)>1\).  After the first full mask expansion, the
cubic row \(c'm=n/r\) is squarefree and is allowed its source \(S\)-part.
The resulting hybrid bound is
\[
 (KNBL)^\epsilon(K+NB)B\{N+L+(NL)^{2/3}\}.
\]
The exact full coefficient from reflection is
\[
 -v/2-\ell_b-e_\lambda/3-(S_0+B_0+z_a)/2.
\]
The hybrid norm, followed by squaring the already extracted common
kernel scalar, gives
\[
 O/2+\max(H,v+\ell_b)-S_0-B_0+z_a-u-\ell_b-2e_\lambda/3
 -\tfrac12(T_d-v-3\ell_b-e_\lambda)_+.
\]
Every fixed part is counted once.  Powerful parts cost \(Z^{O/2+\epsilon}\),
supported valuation-one parts cost a divisor power in
\(\operatorname{rad}(Sf\mathfrak r_\rho)\).
Local width charges are \(2,1,1,-2,0\), bounded by
\((q_k^2/q_{k_{\rm pow}})q_fq_{\mathfrak r_\rho}\).
This proves uniformity for growing frozen \(f\) and punctures.
The actual residual cutoff only gives \(H\le M-O+O(1/\log Z)\).

In the terminal completion of the canonical recursion,
freezing \(h\) leaves slot coefficient \(a_i(p)1_{p\nmid h}\).
It is independent of the current \(f,k\).
The factor \(\Psi_k(h)^3\) is a contraction on the joint
Hilbert space, and \(\sum_hq_h^{-1}\) on a dyad is bounded.
Apply the energy for each fixed \(f\) with uniform constants.
Then \(\sum_fw(f)\ll Z^{V+\epsilon}\) cancels the outside
\(Z^{-V}\) exactly once.  This supplies the full terminal input
for source Lemma 17.2, not only a low-probe special case.

\section{Real-box bounds, fixed contour losses, and scale choice}

The principal tuple estimate is
\[
 |\mathfrak H_{\eta,1,Z}(s,w,z)|
       \ll_{\mathrm{fixed},\mathcal K} Z^{\ell\Re z}
\]
on each fixed bounded real box \(\mathcal K\) in (P), uniformly
in imaginary parts.  It is not uniform as \(\Re z\to+\infty\).
After extracting \(P_i^{\Re z}\) the annular weights retain
\((q_p/P_i)^{\Re z-1}\); supports strictly above one make a
constant uniform on the full unbounded real region impossible.
The fixed \(z_\infty=4000000\) is nonetheless a legitimate
real-box choice.  Its enormous fixed constants do not affect
the final exponent comparison.

On the dynamic lines the numerator functional equation costs
\(U^{a-1/2+6e+\epsilon}\).  Euler restorations on
\(\Re w\ge-6e\) cost at most \(U^{6e+\epsilon}\).
For each strict selected label \(j_p\ge2\), the same single
conductor deficit pays
\[
 -\Re w-(j_p-1)(a-1/2+6e)\le-1/2 .
\]
The explicit aggregate error is therefore \(12ed\) in the
\(Z\)-exponent at \(U=Z^d\), not a freely chosen small power
at fixed \(e\).  The full dynamic loss used in the answer is
\[
 (16-6l_y+12d)e+(1+d)\epsilon_c'
                                    +d\epsilon_d+\epsilon_p .
\]
Choose \(e\) after fixing the slots and before fixing internal
height orders.  The free \(\epsilon_c'\) is separate.

For slot selection order by decreasing amplitude \(g_i\).
A fractional prefix has weighted mean at least the mean of the
whole list including zero amplitudes.  If capacity exceeds the
positive support length, use all positive slots.  Deleting the
one last fractional slot costs at most
\(\delta\max_iw_i\) in the squared spike exponent.
No rearrangement by the physical prime index is involved.

For Mellin inversion the integrable inverse Fourier function is
\(u\mapsto e^{-C(2)u}f_\eta(e^u)\), not the unweighted
\(f_\eta(e^u)\).  The endpoint growth gives its \(L^1\) norm,
and Gaussian decay gives the transform's \(L^1\) norm.

\section{Source receipts and computational scope}

The round-3 worker handoff was inspected member by member.
Only the listed mathematical sources, manifests, notes and
scripts were extracted; no cache or font files were unpacked.
All four user-pinned files rehashed correctly.  The three
additional original PDFs also matched their observed worker
manifests.  Dunn--Radziwill v3 source
\texttt{output.tex} has 263959 bytes and observed SHA256
\begin{verbatim}
47d01be0662e77cf7a8c64c277945f3e54b9d8868ca5f67668647032aa4a2b66
\end{verbatim}
The source archive receipt has 68618 bytes and digest
\begin{verbatim}
1373df1737fb6b70a82a452fdf37658893ab4112f8c000f49bbdd12286f87577
\end{verbatim}
These are observed provenance, not extra user-supplied pins.
The browser had failed to fetch that source archive, but the
worker's direct HTTPS retrieval succeeded.  A browser request
for the Heath--Brown manuscript also failed in the present
environment; its already retrieved and rehashed original PDF
was used instead.  Nothing was reconstructed from an inaccessible
source.

The local environment has pdflatex, pdftotext, pdfinfo and SymPy,
but not Sage.  The supplementary Sage finite-field script and
its worker certificate are preserved below.  The separate SymPy
script was run locally; all 63 named exact identities/inequalities
and both traversals of the eight residue states passed.
It also rehashes all eight retained primary artifacts.
No analytic estimate is inferred from finite enumeration.
The manuscript's exact polynomial identity covers the continuous
unbounded parameter task via its displayed nonnegative
decomposition, and the local multiplicity inequalities cover
all positive integer multiplicities by explicit residual formulas.

The main document uses exactly 12-point article and fullpage,
with no manual layout, font-size or line-spacing change.
It compiles without unresolved references or overfull boxes.
The current allowance is 200 pages, not the older 16-page cap.

\subsection{Locally executable exact-check script}
Run \texttt{python code/audit\_round4.py} from the audit-root
directory containing \texttt{papers/}, \texttt{code/}, and
\texttt{data/}.  The script is standalone apart from SymPy and
the explicitly hashed sources.

\begin{verbatim}
"""Exact checks for the extended moment and descent proof; no analytic theorem is certified.

Run from the audit root: python code/audit_round4.py
All identities use exact SymPy rational expressions, not numerical sampling.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib
import json
import sympy as sp
QQ=sp.Rational

out = {"scope": "Exact algebra and finite residue-state identities only; see the audit report for analytic dependencies."}
checks = []

def ring(names):
    return None, sp.symbols(names)

def ident(name, lhs, rhs=0):
    assert sp.factor(lhs-rhs) == 0, (name, sp.factor(lhs-rhs))
    checks.append(name)

# Section 17: complete local state table, including the marked zero state.
states = []
for parity in (0, 1):
    for a1 in (0, 1):
        for a2 in (0, 1):
            t = (a1-a2+3*parity) % 6
            e1 = (4*a1 + (t != 0)*(1+t) + parity*(a1+a2)) % 6
            e2 = (4*a2 + (t != 0)*(1-t) + parity*(a1+a2)) % 6
            target = 4 if parity or a1*a2 else 0
            assert e1 == e2 == target
            assert (t != 0)-a1-a2+parity*(a1+a2) == parity-2*(not parity)*a1*a2
            states.append([parity, a1, a2, t, e1, e2])
out["section17_complete_local_states"] = states
checks.append("8 local states in L10262-10272 and actual-norm identity L10333-10334")

R, (m,r,ell,V,A,B,t,g,th,v,j,delt,rad) = ring("m r ell V A B t g th v j delt rad")
F = r+3*ell+V
s = r-A-B-t
Hc = 2*r-2*B-m+4*ell+2*V+delt-j
kap = m-2*r-2*ell-V-delt+A+B-rad/2
lc = Hc-th-(s-g)
Cc = ell+rad/2-j+t+g-th
Dc = ell+A+t+g-th+V
Nc = r-A-B-t-g-v
Vc = B+th+v+j
Fc = Nc+Vc
Mc = 2*(s-g)-Hc+delt+2*th
ident("17.48 F transition", Fc, F-Dc-2*ell+j)
ident("17.48 M transition", Mc, m-2*Dc-2*ell+j)
ident("17.48 first margin", Fc-Mc, F-m+Dc)
ident("17.48 second margin", 4*Fc-3*Mc, 4*F-3*m+2*(A+t+g-th+V)+j)
ident("17.64 prefactor", kap+lc+Cc+2*Fc, F)
ident("17.39 principal ledger", kap+Hc+s+B+t+ell+rad/2, F-B-j)
ident("17.65 perturbation coefficient", QQ(9)/2+18+QQ(11)/2+2*6, 40)

R, (m,r,z,G,P) = ring("m r z G P")
Dp=r+z-2*G
z0=z-G
Fi=Dp-P
Mi=2*Dp-m-2*P
ki=m-2*Dp+P
ident("17 initialization first invariant", Fi-Mi-(P+G)-z0, m-r-2*z+2*G)
ident("17 initialization second invariant", 4*Fi-3*Mi-6*z0, 3*m-2*r-8*z+10*G+2*P)
ident("17 initialization energy", ki+P+2*Fi, m)

# Section 18: identities are checked before max/positive-part inequalities.
R, (m,q,A,c,d,p,Rr,E,K,w,wo,gg,ll,c2,d2,p2,g2,t2,V,f,Bc,Bd,s0) = ring(
    "m q A c d p Rr E K w wo gg ll c2 d2 p2 g2 t2 V f Bc Bd s0")
M=m+q
qt=q+Rr+E
K0=2*A-c-d+Rr+E-m
a0=A-c-w
J=d-c+(K0-K)-2*w+wo
mp=2*a0-K-gg-g2-V
qp=qt+wo+t2+V
Mp=mp+qp
b2=(c2+d2)/2
allow=b2-p2+w+Bc+ll
ident("18.32 child width", Mp, M+J-gg-g2+t2)
ident("unnumbered first aggregate ledger after 18.23",
      m-A-Rr/2-E+p+s0+(A-c+qt+Bc-s0+A-d+qt+Bd-s0)/2,
      M+(Bc+Bd-(c+d-2*p-Rr))/2)
ident("18.31 diagonal deficit",
      K+gg-ll-s0-(a0+qt+wo+w+Bc-s0),
      A-M-d-(K0-K)-wo-Bc+gg-ll)
ledger=K+gg-ll-a0+p2-s0+g2-t2-b2
ident("18.33 child allowance", a0+qt+wo+w+Bc-s0-ledger, Mp+allow)
F1=c/6+5*(d+K0-K)/6+w/3+qt/6+wo+Bc-5*gg/6+ll
F2=2*b2-5*g2/6-p2+t2+V/6+f/6
ident("18.42 exceptional deficit", (mp-f)/6+a0-b2-(Mp+allow), A-5*M/6-F1-F2)
ident("18.38 affine child difference",
      (A-c-w-c2-Mp)-(A-M),
      gg+w-d-c2-(K0-K)-wo+g2-t2)

# Local infinite families in F2: displayed residual polynomials are
# nonnegative on their stated domains. These are symbolic equalities.
R, (i,jj) = ring("i jj")
ident("F2 unit residual", 7*i/6-2*i/3, i/2)
ident("F2 nonunit i>=2 residual", (7*i-5)/6-2*i/3, (3*i-5)/6)
ident("F2 nonunit i=1 equality", QQ(7-5)/6+QQ(1)/3, QQ(2)/3)
ident("F2 equal six-divisible residual", 7*i/6-1-2*i/3, (i-2)/2)
ident("F2 unequal residual", i+jj/6-1-(i+jj)/3, (4*i-jj-6)/6)

# Full-range row envelopes and exact parameter bounds.
R, (delta,x,t,Delta,r,mm,lam) = ring("delta x t Delta r mm lam")
alpha=QQ(5)/6
D=3-17*x/9
P=(2-8*x/9)*(1-x)
Jj=(alpha-delta)*D+delta*P
rs=((2-8*x/9)*t-5*x/9)/D
AI=1-delta*(x+(1-x)*r)
St=1-delta*(4*x/9+(2-8*x/9)*(t-r))
Rshort=1-delta+delta*P/D*(QQ(3)/2-t)
ident("19.7 weighted crossing", ((2-8*x/9)*AI+(1-x)*St)/D, Rshort)
ident("rstar upper endpoint", rs.subs({t:QQ(3)/2}), 1)
ident("rstar lower-endpoint margin", rs.subs({t:1})-QQ(23)/37,
      (1-2*x)*5/(37*D))
ident("plain crossing minimum margin", (t-rs).subs({t:1})-QQ(1)/3,
      5*x/(27*D))
topt=1+delta*P/(2*Jj)
Rstar=1-delta+(alpha-delta)*delta*P/(2*Jj)
ident("optimal adaptive short envelope", Rshort.subs({t:topt}), Rstar)
ident("optimal adaptive long envelope", 1-delta+(alpha-delta)*(topt-1), Rstar)
capdiff=2*(x*delta)*(1-2*mm)*(QQ(2)/9-1/(QQ(9)/2+12*Delta))
ident("19.6 exact capacity change", capdiff,
      24*x*delta*(1-2*mm)*Delta/((QQ(9)/2)*(QQ(9)/2+12*Delta)))
# On delta<=5/6, x<=1/2, mm>=1/3, Delta>=0,
# capdiff <= (40/243) Delta < Delta/4 (at exact lengths).
capratio=QQ(24)*(QQ(5)/12)*(QQ(1)/3)/(QQ(9)/2)**2
assert capratio==QQ(40)/243 < QQ(1)/4
out["capacity_increment_over_Delta_bound_exact_lengths"] = str(capratio)
ident("large-bin amplification excess", (1+5*r)/6-delta*r-(1-delta), (alpha-delta)*(r-1))
ident("unselected t=1 crossing", 1-delta*QQ(2)/3, 1-2*delta/3)
ell=QQ(1)/6+lam
h=QQ(13)/16+3*lam/2
zeta=QQ(1)/10**6
ident("supply inequality numerator", 5*ell-h-zeta, QQ(1)/48+7*lam/2-zeta)
assert QQ(1)/48-zeta>0
out["minimum_supply_at_lambda0_dmax"] = str((QQ(1)/6)/(QQ(13)/16+zeta))
out["marked_second_margin_lower_bound"] = "9/37"
out["floor_unweighted_claimed_exponent_if_misapplied"] = str(1-QQ(2)/3*QQ(1)/50)


# Main descent: exact identities on all real parameter ranges.
lam, delta, x, y, d, RR, ss, BB, ee, eps = sp.symbols("lam delta x y d RR ss BB ee eps")
ell=QQ(1)/6+lam
lx=QQ(17)/48-lam/2
ly=QQ(23)/48-lam/2
h=QQ(13)/16+3*lam/2
theta=QQ(7)/8-lam/4
z0=QQ(17)/50
ident("geometry difference", ly-lx, QQ(1)/8)
ident("geometry sum", lx+ly, 1-ell)
ident("geometry high length", 1-lx+ell, h)
ident("C independent of deformation", ss+lx/2-1+h/6, ss-QQ(11)/16)
ident("low versus theta", lx/2+QQ(1)/96, theta-QQ(11)/16)

alpha=QQ(5)/6
D=3-17*x/9
PP=(2-8*x/9)*(1-x)
JJ=(alpha-delta)*D+delta*PP
Rstar=1-delta+(alpha-delta)*delta*PP/(2*JJ)
ident("D minus P positivity polynomial",D-PP,1+x-8*x*x/9)
ident("J minimum corner",JJ.subs({delta:alpha,x:QQ(1)/2}),QQ(35)/54)
ident("J maximum corner",JJ.subs({delta:0,x:0}),QQ(5)/2)

a=(1+delta)/2
def endpoint(dval,qval,Rval):
    return a-theta+h*(z0-QQ(1)/6)-a*ly-ell/2+qval*ell+dval*(Rval+delta/2-z0)
E0=endpoint(QQ(13)/16,x*delta,Rstar).subs(lam,0)
ident("tight endpoint representation",E0,
      -QQ(1)/48+2*delta/3+x*delta/6-QQ(13)/16*(1-Rstar))
JY=JJ.subs(x,QQ(1)/2-y)
E0Y=E0.subs(x,QQ(1)/2-y)
vv=51+41*y
certificate_rhs=(3+5*y)*((4*vv*delta-79)**2+49)+4*y*(
    4*vv*delta*((1+3*y)*(15+32*y)*delta+9-13*y)+265+3485*y)
ident("continuous exact endpoint certificate",10368*vv*JY*(-E0Y),certificate_rhs)
ident("exact perturbed endpoint",endpoint(h,x*delta,Rstar)-E0,
      lam*(-QQ(1)/4+(1+x)*delta+QQ(3)/2*Rstar))

ident("floor endpoint",endpoint(h,delta/2,1).subs(delta,QQ(1)/50),
      -QQ(7)/1200+32*lam/25)
mid=-QQ(529)/2400+25*delta/96+lam*(QQ(13)/50+3*delta/4)
ident("middle exact endpoint",endpoint(QQ(1)/2,delta/2,QQ(76)/75-2*delta/3),mid)
ident("middle max endpoint",mid.subs(delta,alpha),-QQ(49)/14400+177*lam/200)
ident("middle d slope", QQ(76)/75-2*delta/3+delta/2-z0,QQ(101)/150-delta/6)
ident("large bin endpoint",endpoint(h,delta/2,1-delta),
      -QQ(1)/48-delta/16+5*lam/4)

margin=QQ(49)/440640-QQ(5)/(2*10**6)
ident("exact rational reserved margin",margin,QQ(59873)/550800000)
assert margin-QQ(2)/10**6 > QQ(1)/10000
theta_new=theta.subs(lam,QQ(1)/10**6)
assert QQ(1)/2<=theta_new<QQ(7)/8
ident("target theta",theta_new,QQ(3499999)/4000000)
ident("strict improvement",QQ(7)/8-theta_new,QQ(1)/4000000)
out["theta"]=str(theta_new)
out["improvement"]=str(QQ(7)/8-theta_new)
out["endpoint_margin"]=str(margin)
out["margin_after_extension"]=str(margin-QQ(2)/10**6)
out["baseline_endpoint_certificate_residual"]="0"

# Contour exponents, including explicit 12e conductor/deletion cost.
def raw(s,z,w):
    return lx/2+s-1+h*z+ly*(w-1)
ident("principal w contour",raw(BB+ee,QQ(1)/6+ee,QQ(19)/20)-(BB-QQ(11)/16),
      (1+h)*ee-ly/20)
ident("principal z contour",raw(BB+ee,QQ(33)/200,1)-(BB-QQ(11)/16),
      ee-h/600)
ident("small rows exponent",raw(BB+ee,z0,QQ(1)/2)+QQ(2)/100-(BB-QQ(11)/16),
      -QQ(63)/800+QQ(51)/100*lam+ee)
zeta=QQ(1)/10**6
B0=lx/2+1+ly
tail_exp=B0+(h+zeta)*(1+eps)-zeta*4000000+eps
tail_worst=tail_exp.subs({lam:QQ(1)/10**6,eps:QQ(1)/10**6})
assert tail_worst < -1
ident("unbounded tail endpoint value",tail_worst,-QQ(612498574999)/400000000000)
out["unbounded_tail_maximum"]=str(tail_worst)
ident("dynamic physical extra e loss",
      (a+16*ee-theta)+h*(z0-QQ(1)/6)+ly*(1-a-6*ee-1)-ell/2
      +x*delta*ell+d*(RR+delta/2+12*ee-z0)-endpoint(d,x*delta,RR),
      (16-6*ly+12*d)*ee)

# Rational selected local factor identities.
D,V,W,R,TT,Q=sp.symbols("D V W R TT Q", nonzero=True)
Ec=(R*((1-1/Q)/(1-V)+W-D)-TT*V/(1-V))/(1-R)
Pstar=-D+Ec
Ptot=1/(1-V)+W/(1-W)+Pstar
Hloc=(1-V)*(1-W)*Ptot/(1-D)
Gloc=((1/D-W)*(1-V)*(1-W)*Pstar-W*(1-V*W))/(1-D)
ident("unselected principal local algebra",Hloc-1,
      (D*(V+W-V*W)-V*W+(1-V)*(1-W)*Ec)/(1-D))
ident("selected principal local cancellation",Gloc+Hloc,
      (1-W)*(Hloc-1+(V-D)/(1-D))+(1-V)*(1-W)*Ec/(D*(1-D)))
defect=QQ(336)*225/4/10**8
ident("prime ideal defect sum",defect,QQ(189)/10**6)
ident("principal contraction",2*defect,QQ(189)/500000)
assert 2*defect<QQ(1)/2

# Energy accounting and recursion's displayed rational identities.
O,H,A,N,S,G,z,vc,tc,ev,ellc,dd,vart,Gam=sp.symbols(
    "O H A N S G z vc tc ev ellc dd vart Gam")
Mp=1-ellc-2*dd
ellp=ellc-dd
Td=2*H+2*A+2*z-1-ellp-vart-N-3*G
ident("low energy width identity",Td-(H-3*dd),
      H-Mp+2*A+2*(z-ellp)-N-3*G-vart)
ident("low energy branch identity",
      (O/2+Gam+S+G+Td/4).subs(H,Mp-O-Gam),
      (2*Mp+2*z-1-ellp+2*Gam-vart+2*A-N+G+4*S)/4)
ident("low energy deformation term",1+3*ellp-2*Mp,5*ellc+dd-1)

# Independent exact maximality checks for piecewise-linear local deficits.
i,jj=sp.symbols("i jj",integer=True)
for a1 in [0,1]:
    for a2 in [0,1]:
        for parity in [0,1]:
            t=(a1-a2+3*parity)%6
            c1=(4*a1+(1 if t else 0)*(1+t)+parity*(a1+a2))%6
            c2=(4*a2+(1 if t else 0)*(1-t)+parity*(a1+a2))%6
            assert c1==c2==(4 if parity or a1*a2 else 0)
out["all_eight_canonical_states_rechecked"]=True

# Source integrity: four user pins, plus observed provenance for original sources.
pins=[]
for f in json.loads(Path("papers/pinned/manifest.json").read_text()):
    pp=Path("papers/pinned")/f["filename"]
    digest=hashlib.sha256(pp.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    assert digest==f["expected_sha256"]
    pins.append({"file":str(pp),"bytes":pp.stat().st_size,"sha256":digest,
                 "authority":"user pin"})
for f in json.loads(Path("papers/originals/manifest.json").read_text()):
    pp=Path("papers/originals")/f["file"]
    digest=hashlib.sha256(pp.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    assert digest==f["sha256"]
    pins.append({"file":str(pp),"bytes":pp.stat().st_size,"sha256":digest,
                 "authority":"observed original-source manifest, not user pin"})
pp=Path("papers/originals/dr-v3-source/output.tex")
digest=hashlib.sha256(pp.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
assert digest=="47d01be0662e77cf7a8c64c277945f3e54b9d8868ca5f67668647032aa4a2b66"
pins.append({"file":str(pp),"bytes":pp.stat().st_size,"sha256":digest,
             "authority":"observed worker source-retrieval digest"})
out["sources_rehashed"]=pins
out["identity_checks"]=checks
out["identity_count"]=len(checks)
out["all_exact_checks_passed"]=True
out["scope"]="Exact identities and rational inequalities only. The analytic and arithmetic proofs are in answer.tex."
Path("data/round4_exact_certificates.json").write_text(json.dumps(out,indent=2)+"\n")
print(json.dumps({"all_exact_checks_passed":True,"identity_count":len(checks),
                  "certificate":"data/round4_exact_certificates.json"},indent=2))

\end{verbatim}
