\subsection{Finite-field and cusp-construction script}
The following worker script requires Sage.  Its finite tests are
supplementary to the arbitrary-prime proof, not a proof by sampling.
\begin{verbatim}
"""Exact finite checks for the round-3 reflection audit.

Run with: sage -python code/audit_round3.sage.py
These are algebra certificates, not verification of automorphy or an analytic theorem.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import json
from sage.all import (CyclotomicField, QQ, ZZ, PolynomialRing, matrix, prod)

K = CyclotomicField(3)
w = K.gen()
O = K.ring_of_integers()
lam = 1 + 2*w
units = [s*w**j for s in (1,-1) for j in range(3)]
records = {"scope": "Exact finite algebra checks; standard automorphy is imported."}

def integral(x):
    return x in O

def congruent(a,b,m):
    return integral((a-b)/m)

def primary(x):
    return next(u*x for u in units if congruent(u*x,1,3))

def e_exponent(x):
    # Tr(x/lambda) is the omega coefficient of x in Q(omega).
    return QQ(K(x)[1])

# Exact local Fourier identities, including x=0, for split and inert good primes.
local = []
for p in [1+3*w, 4+3*w, -2+3*w, K(-5)]:
    k = O.ideal(p).residue_field()
    q = ZZ(k.cardinality())
    ell = ZZ(k.characteristic())
    C = CyclotomicField(6*ell)
    z6, zell = C.gen()**ell, C.gen()**6
    sixth_roots = [(-w**2)**j for j in range(6)]
    reduction_to_power = {k(u):j for j,u in enumerate(sixth_roots)}
    def chi(x,j):
        if not x:
            return C(0)
        return z6**(j*reduction_to_power[x**((q-1)//6)])
    def additive(x):
        t = e_exponent(K(k.lift(x))/p)
        assert (ell*t).denominator() == 1
        return zell**ZZ(ell*t)
    elts = list(k)
    nonzero = [x for x in elts if x]
    gm = {j:sum((chi(y,j)*additive(-y) for y in elts),C(0)) for j in range(1,6)}
    gp = {j:sum((chi(y,j)*additive(y) for y in elts),C(0)) for j in range(1,6)}
    for j in range(1,6):
        assert gm[j]*gm[j].conjugate() == q
        assert gp[j]*gp[j].conjugate() == q
    fourier = {(j,h):sum((chi(y,j)*additive(-h*y) for y in elts),C(0))/q
               for j in range(6) for h in elts}
    for j in range(6):
        for h in elts:
            expected = (gm[j]*chi(h,-j)/q if h else C(0)) if j else (QQ(-1)/q if h else 1-QQ(1)/q)
            assert fourier[j,h] == expected
    count = 0
    for eps in [k(1), k.multiplicative_generator()]:
        for j in range(6):
            for x in elts:
                lhs = sum((fourier[j,h]*chi(h,-2)*additive(eps*x/h) for h in nonzero),C(0))
                if j == 4:
                    rhs = gm[4]/q*(-1+q*(x==0))
                elif j == 0:
                    rhs = -gp[2]/q*chi(eps*x,-2)
                else:
                    rhs = gm[j]*gp[(j+2)%6]/q*chi(eps*x,-j-2)
                assert lhs == rhs
                count += 1
    local.append({"prime":str(p),"norm":int(q),"fourier_entries":6*int(q),
                  "active_branch_checks":count,"gauss_norm_checks":10})
records["local_fourier"] = local

# Source Gauss sum at the excluded primary prime -2 has magnitude sqrt(4).
k = O.ideal(-2).residue_field()
C = CyclotomicField(6); z6=C.gen()
cubics={k(w**j):j for j in range(3)}
g=C(0)
for x in k:
    if x:
        t=QQ((K(k.lift(x))/(-2)).trace())
        assert (6*t).denominator()==1
        g += z6**(2*cubics[x])*z6**ZZ(6*t)
assert g*g.conjugate()==4
records["source_prime_minus_2"]={"norm":4,"gauss_squared_absolute_value":4}

# The full h0 range modulo L=18, with all three ramified cusp cases.
# Actual enormous CRT lifts are kept only in memory. No database is produced.
L=K(18); M=lam**12*L**4
primes=[1+3*w,4+3*w]
fixed_primes=[K(-2),lam]

def crt_ideals(residues, moduli):
    value=K(0); current=O.ideal(1)
    for a,I in zip(residues,moduli):
        value += (a-value)*current.element_1_mod(I)
        current *= I
        value = current.small_residue(value)
    return K(value)

hist={0:0,1:0,2:0}
checks=0
for h_a in range(18):
    for h_b in range(18):
        h0=K(h_a+h_b*w)
        d=O.ideal(L,lam**2*h0).gens_reduced()[0]
        aF,cF=lam**2*h0/d,L/d
        norm_target=aF if congruent(cF,0,lam) else cF
        u=next(u for u in units if congruent(u*norm_target,1,3))
        aF,cF=u*aF,u*cF
        hcase=min(2,int(O.ideal(cF).valuation(O.ideal(lam))))
        hist[hcase]+=1
        for active in [[],primes[:1],primes]:
            r=prod(active,K(1)); c=cF*r
            previous=None
            for frequency in [1,2]:
                hp={p:crt_ideals([K(frequency),K(0)],[O.ideal(p),O.ideal(M**2)]) for p in active}
                a=aF*r+lam**2*cF*sum(((r/p)*hp[p] for p in active),K(0))
                assert O.ideal(a,c)==O.ideal(1)
                residues=[]; moduli=[]
                for fp in fixed_primes:
                    I=O.ideal(fp); ec=int(O.ideal(cF).valuation(I)); em=int(O.ideal(M).valuation(I))
                    modulus=I**(em+ec if ec else em)
                    if ec:
                        # y*a = 1 modulo this prime-power modulus.
                        y=O.ideal(a).element_1_mod(modulus)/a
                        residues.append(y)
                    else:
                        residues.append(K(0))
                    moduli.append(modulus)
                for p in active:
                    I=O.ideal(p)
                    residues.append(O.ideal(a).element_1_mod(I)/a)
                    moduli.append(I)
                delta=crt_ideals(residues,moduli)
                b=(a*delta-1)/c
                assert integral(b)
                g=matrix(K,[[a,b],[c,delta]])
                assert g.det()==1
                if hcase==2:
                    H=matrix(K,[[1,0],[0,1]])
                elif hcase==1:
                    u0=next(z for z in [lam,-lam] if congruent(z,c,3))
                    H=matrix(K,[[1,0],[u0,1]])
                else:
                    u0=next(K(s+t*w) for s in range(3) for t in range(3) if congruent(s+t*w,a,3))
                    H=matrix(K,[[u0,-1],[1,0]])
                G=g*H.inverse()
                assert all(congruent(G[i,j],int(i==j),3) for i in range(2) for j in range(2))
                fixed=tuple(O.ideal(M).small_residue(z) for z in [a,b,c,delta])
                if previous is not None:
                    assert fixed==previous
                previous=fixed
                DF=lam**3*cF
                rinv=O.ideal(r).element_1_mod(O.ideal(DF))/r
                for x in [K(1),w,1+w]:
                    phase=-delta*rinv*x/DF
                    for p in active:
                        kp=O.ideal(p).residue_field()
                        sigma=lam**2*c/p
                        eps=-1/kp((lam**3*c/p)*sigma)
                        phase+=K(kp.lift(eps/kp(hp[p])))*x/p
                    assert integral(phase+delta*x/(DF*r))
                checks+=1
records["crt_cusp"]={"fixed_modulus":"L=18, M=lambda^12 L^4",
                      "h0_classes":324,"ramified_cusp_case_counts":hist,
                      "constructions":checks,"phase_checks":3*checks,
                      "active_radicals":["1",str(primes[0]),str(prod(primes))],
                      "frequencies_per_radical":2}

# Symbolic identities behind local pair phases and norm exponents.
R=PolynomialRing(QQ,names='v ell e S B z H O Td'.split())
v,ell,e,S,B,z,H,oo,Td=R.gens()
outside=-v-2*ell-2*e/3-S-B
for row_term in [H,v+ell]:
    for cubic_term,u in [(v,z),(z,v),(2*(v+z)/3,(v+z)/3)]:
        assert outside+row_term+ell+cubic_term == row_term-S-B+z-u-ell-2*e/3
records["energy_exponent_identities"]=6
records["pair_phase_cubic_exponents"]={"row_mark":(1+0+2)%3,"row_fourth_power":(1+4+2)%3}
assert records["pair_phase_cubic_exponents"]=={"row_mark":0,"row_fourth_power":1}

# Treat conjugate variables as independent for the horizontal derivative.
A=PolynomialRing(QQ,names='z zb a ab c cb delta deltab v'.split())
z,zb,a,ab,c,cb,delta,deltab,v=A.gens()
F=A.fraction_field()
b=(a*delta-1)/c
Z=z+a/c; Zb=zb+ab/cb
den=(-c*Z+a)*(-cb*Zb+ab)+c*cb*v**2
horizontal=((delta*Z-b)*(-cb*Zb+ab)-delta*cb*v**2)/den
assert horizontal == -delta/c-zb/(c**2*(v**2+z*zb))
assert horizontal.derivative(zb).subs(z=0,zb=0) == -1/(c**2*v**2)
conjugate=-deltab/cb-z/(cb**2*(v**2+z*zb))
vertical=v/den
assert conjugate.derivative(zb).subs(z=0,zb=0)==0
assert vertical.derivative(zb).subs(z=0,zb=0)==0
records['horizontal_coordinate_and_derivatives']=4

A=PolynomialRing(QQ,names=['t']); t=A.gen(); s=QQ(1)/2-t
assert 4*s-2==-4*t
assert -3*s-QQ(5)/2==-4+3*t
assert 1-2*s==2*t
assert s-1==-QQ(1)/2-t
assert QQ(3)/2-s==1+t
assert s+QQ(1)/3==1-t-QQ(1)/6
assert s+QQ(2)/3==1-t+QQ(1)/6
records['mellin_gamma_and_scalar_exponent_identities']=7

out=Path('data/round3_exact_certificates.json')
out.write_text(json.dumps(records,indent=2)+'\n')
print(json.dumps(records,indent=2))

\end{verbatim}

\section{Exact probe and detector working definitions}
Fix \(T\) to be the ray class group modulo \((36)\).
The primary mod-\(4\) table and the supplement at \(2\) make
\(G,\mathcal R\) functions of this fixed group.  The target-dependent
fixed numerator \(\Xi(a)=\xi(a)\chi_a(b_*)\) need not factor through
\(T\), but its conductor support lies in \(S\).
Choose \(S\) once after the buffer \(e\), with
\[
 P_0\ge\max(10^{10},(2C_e)^{1/(10e)})
\]
and all target defining-modulus primes included.
The choice \(P_0=10^{10}\) by itself was only the principal
contraction cutoff; the dynamic lower bound for \(H_p\) may require
a much larger cutoff when \(e\) is small.

The corrected completed row is
\[
 \sum_{\substack{c\ {\rm sf}\\n}}
 \gamma_2(c)\overline{\alpha(cn^3)}
 \eta(cn^3)\Xi(cn^3)^{-1}\mathcal R(cn^3,s)
 \chi_{cn^3}(m)\overline{G(cn^3)}
 q_c^{-1/2-t}q_n^{-1-3t}.
\]
Its physical outer factors are
\[
 \frac1Y\sum_s
 \frac{W_{\rm b,1}(q_s/Y)\chi_s(b_*)}{\tau\xi(s)\sqrt{q_{b_*}q_sX}}
 \sum_m \xi(m)q_s^{-1/2}g_{\chi_s}(s,-m)
           W_{\rm b,0}(q_m/(q_{b_*}q_sX)).
\]
The \(t\)-integral has weight \(Z^t e^{t^2}\) on \(\Re t=4\).
A whole-index mark means \(D\mid cn^3\), not \(D\mid c\).
The low identity is exact Mellin separation, while Poisson on the
full modulus \(sb_*cn^3\) gives the high identity.  No coprimality
between \(s\) and \(cn^3\) is assumed.

The finite coefficient before its scalar Euler product is
\[
 F(s,A,H)=
 \sum_{\substack{d\bmod s\\H\equiv b_*Ad\pmod s}}
       \chi_s(d)\,g_{\xi\chi_A}\!
                \left(b_*A,\frac{H-b_*Ad}{s}\right).
\]
Primitive \(\xi\) removes every \(H\) meeting \(S\), including zero.
Writing \(H=ua^6\) yields the scalar factor
\[
 \frac{\zeta_F^S(6z)L_F^S(w,\chi_\bullet(u))}
              {L_F^S(x,\eta\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)})}.
\]
The selected operation is exactly
\[
 \overline{\eta(p)}Q^{x+z-1}P_p^*-Q^{z-w-1}P_p,
\]
not a quotient assumed regular at zeros of \(P_p\).
Its quotient-free correction is printed in the answer.

For the detector, the bins use the maximum of zero real parts in
nested heights \(3iT_1\), with floor \(51/100\).
A zero at \(\rho=\sigma+i\gamma\) gives the truncated inverse
at \(D_*=U^t\), \(1\le t\le3/2\), and gamma smoothing at
\(Y_*=U^{20}\).
Shifting only that entire \(L\)-integral to \(-1/4\) gives
\[
 J\ll U^{-3+t(5/4-\sigma)+o(1)}T_1^2
      \le U^{-189/100+o(1)}T_1^2=o(1).
\]
The unit term in the expanded convolution is \(1+o(1)\), so its
tail has nonzero mass.  A common Fourier separation of the product
cutoff produces one character, one height and one dyadic pair
with product spike
\[
 |M_rS_m|^2\gg U^{\delta(r+m)-\epsilon},
 \qquad r+m\ge t-O(1/\log U),\quad r\le t+O(1/\log U).
\]
The pointwise bounds for the same profiles have exponents
\(\delta r\) and \(\delta\min(m,1-m)\).
Therefore \(2\delta(m-1/2)_+\ll\epsilon\), hence
\(m\le1/2+O(\epsilon)\) since \(\delta\ge1/50\).
Dividing the product lower bound by each individual upper bound
gives both saturated spikes.  The detector uses the entire
pre-saturation bounded range \(m\le21+o(1)\) when choosing its
smooth and height orders.

\section{Canonical two-transform reconstruction data}
The canonical coefficient is
\(a_0(n)=\bar\alpha(n)\gamma_2(n)\), multiplied only by a
fixed finite character, a fixed puncture, the row symbol,
a squarefree averaged fourth-power label, and a product-form mark.
It has no arbitrary residual bounded column coefficient.

The reopened nonterminal block is
\[
 Z^{-r-2l-V}\sum_f w(f)\sum_k\Phi(q_k/Z^M)
 \left|\sum_b\beta(b,f)\chi_b(k)^3
       \sum_{n\ {\rm sf}}a_0(n)\nu(n)\rho(n)
          \chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4
          \mathfrak d(nb^3)W(q_n/Z^r)\right|^2 .
\]
Here \(F=r+3l+V\) and \(V+l\ge d>0\).
For the first transform let
\[
 \mathfrak B=\operatorname{rad}(b_1b_2),\quad
 n_i=\mathfrak A_i C u_i,\quad
 \mathfrak A_i=(n_i,\mathfrak B).
\]
The residual \(u_i\) are mutually coprime and prime to
\(C\mathfrak B\).  With
\(t_p=1_{p\mid\mathfrak A_1}-1_{p\mid\mathfrak A_2}
          +3v_p(b_1b_2)\pmod6\),
\[
 R_1=\prod_{t_p\ne0}p,\quad
 \psi_1=\chi_{u_1}\bar\chi_{u_2}
            \prod_{t_p\ne0}\chi_p^{t_p},\quad
 R_{\rm mask}=C\mathfrak B/R_1.
\]
The exact raw Poisson sum, with \(\beta\), all original column
masks, and \(d_k\mid R_{\rm mask}\), is included in the answer
before its coefficient extraction.

Write
\(b_1b_2=q_0^2J_2^2s\), \(J=sJ_2\).
The eight-state local table gives the common multiplier
\(\chi_n(J)^4 1_{(n,\operatorname{rad}q_0)=1}\).
Put \(E=\prod_{\pi_p=1}p^{a_{1p}+a_{2p}}\) and
\(y=hf^2E\).  The first separated column is exactly
\[
 \mu(x)\nu_i(x)\rho(x)\overline{\chi_x(y)}
 \chi_x(CJ)^4\chi_x(d_k)
 1_{(x,\operatorname{rad}(q_0t'))=1}
 \mathfrak d_{I_i}(x)W_i(q_x/Z^{s_i}).
\]
The old \(f\) is removed only after weighted Cauchy by the divisor
fibre \(f^2E\mid(y)\), never by counting a new free label.

The second transform writes \(x_a=g'z_a\), \(d_2\mid g'\),
then uses the complete residual inversion \(z_a=v'n_a\).
Its exact reindexing is
\[
 k'=d_kd_2k'',\quad f'=JCd_2v',\quad
 \rho'(n)=\rho(n)1_{(n,\operatorname{rad}(q_0t'(g'/d_2)))=1}.
\]
The old zeros imply \(J,C,d_2,v'\) squarefree and pairwise
coprime, before any weights are replaced by upper bounds.
The fixed triple is \((q_0,t',g'/d_2)\); the four factors of
\(f'\) are not fixed and counted again outside the label average.
Their fibre is bounded by
\[
 d_{\mathcal O}(f')^{9+4K}
 d_{\mathcal O}(q_0)^{5+2K}
 d_{\mathcal O}(t')^{2K}
 d_{\mathcal O}(g'/d_2)^{2K}.
\]
The triple set is the projection of the source-supported outer set.
A larger norm ball is used for counting only, not for testing
the child puncture inequality.

The marked initialization freezes \(j=(n,P)\) before squaring,
with the \(Z^{-G}\) overlap weight paying for its choices.
Its first residual product is squarefree, \(c=(n/j)(P/j)\).
After one Poisson transform and complete residual inversion,
\[
 k=d'h',\quad f=d's,\quad \rho_{\rm init}(n)=1_{(n,tj)=1},
 \qquad t=C/d'.
\]
The exact outer zero \(1_{(s,h')=1}\) survives through Cauchy.
The reconstruction fibre has weight
\(d_{\mathcal O}(f)^{1+2K}d_{\mathcal O}(t)^{2K}\),
with \(t\) counted once as a frozen quotient.

\section{Plain allocated coefficient and local-error safeguards}
For every genuine complete-common-support extraction, fix the
exact powers removed from each plain variable and the identities
of the frozen slots.  The residual coefficient is the actual sum
over \(a=l_1l_2\Pi\), with all residual factors avoiding the
extracted radical and the old common mask, and with the entire
rectangle difference \(D_{\mathbf b}(l_1,l_2)\).
This sum definition appears as the allocated-coefficient formula
in the answer.  It makes the reverse reconstruction of original
pairs unique when residual products are coprime.

The two complete Poisson formulas and the full residual M\"obius
allocations preserve one character, one norm power, and one common
mask on each full residual product.  A final divisor prime assigned
to a plain extracts exactly one copy with no new one-variable
puncture.  A divisor prime assigned to a slot freezes that slot.
Equal-product rectangles must not be clipped on exceptional
characters; only the nonexceptional children are first separated
by triangle and then clipped rectangle by rectangle.

For the dynamic local error, the off-row error saves \(Q^{-51/100}\).
All six ramified boundary terms save more than \(Q^{-1/2}\)
after \(Q^z\) is separated.  The unique strict term at \(j\ge2\)
is paid simultaneously using
\[
 q_{\mathfrak f_u}\ll_S q_u
       \prod_{p\in J_0}q_p^{-(j_p-1)},\qquad
 A_*=a-\frac12+6e,\quad w_r=1-a-6e.
\]
For every such prime,
\[
 -w_r-(j_p-1)A_*=-\frac12-(j_p-2)A_*\le-\frac12 .
\]
The numerator and every error slot together cost
\(U^{a-1/2+12e+\epsilon}\prod P_i^{z_r-1/2}\).
There is no additional \(e\)-loss per slot; the \(12e\) is one
aggregate allowance, not a freely shrinkable divisor exponent.

\section{Additional exact checks and reproduction}
The continuous certificate is reproduced in the earlier script section.
The current \texttt{exact\_certificate.py}, normalized to one terminal
newline, has digest
\nolinkurl{af406d0f43d651838a65c39da9eb69c3837fba72f2ddad7c15adb4dc9bc7711f}.
The predecessor script's historical digest is
\nolinkurl{eeaaee4c35841a152fca099008bea71be40575653ece68de02fe07cc8f3ada0b}.
The local certificate below checks exact affine extrema,
all eight local states, the full canonical length identities,
the initialization identities, and the new plain endpoint.
It also checks five quotient-free Euler rational identities.
It is \texttt{local\_transform\_certificate.py}, normalized digest
\nolinkurl{77d9833579f17d533ef6c19d3dc9fbc68243e24b5383ebd158e656c39086fe45}.
Both scripts succeed using rational arithmetic.
They do not verify the analytic Poisson norm inequalities.

The completion-of-the-square inequality is \(F\ge D_v\), with
equality at \(y=0,\delta=\delta_v\).  The strict required estimate
is nevertheless valid because \(D_v>5\cdot10^{-12}\).
The rescaled-subset expression is measured relative to the
nominal low exponent before the unrescaled energy correction.
To measure excess over the actual unrescaled bound, subtract its
value at \(d=0\).  Its maximum still occurs at \(d=0\).

The standalone answer uses exactly the required 12-point article
class and \texttt{fullpage}, with no other margin or line-spacing
changes and no in-document font-size commands.
Reproduction commands are:
\begin{verbatim}
python exact_certificate.py
python local_transform_certificate.py
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error answer.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error answer.tex
\end{verbatim}
The read-only predecessor was recompiled successfully to 153 pages.
Its second pass had no undefined references, LaTeX warnings, or overfull boxes.
The final revised build is recorded with the current receipt below.
\begin{verbatim}
"""Exact local and transformation checks; no analytic theorem is inferred.
Python 3 + SymPy. No network, no floating-point arithmetic.
"""
import sympy as s
Q=s.Rational
a,e=s.symbols("a e", real=True)
vertices=[{a:A,e:E} for A in [Q(51,100),s.Integer(1)]
                         for E in [s.Integer(0),Q(1,1000)]]
off=[-a-4*e,-Q(51,25)+12*e,Q(49,25)-5*a-68*e,
     a-Q(51,25)+12*e]
ram=[a-2+6*e,Q(1,2)-2*a-32*e,-a-26*e,
     1-3*a-48*e,Q(1,2)-2*a-42*e,2-5*a-80*e]
offmax=[max(f.subs(v) for v in vertices) for f in off]
rammax=[max(f.subs(v) for v in vertices) for f in ram]
assert offmax == [-Q(51,100),-Q(507,250),-Q(59,100),-Q(257,250)]
assert rammax == [-Q(497,500),-Q(13,25),-Q(51,100),
                  -Q(53,100),-Q(13,25),-Q(11,20)]
assert all(m <= -Q(51,100) for m in offmax)
assert all(m < -Q(1,2) for m in rammax)
w=1-a-6*e
Astar=a-Q(1,2)+6*e
for j in range(2,6):
    assert s.expand(-w-(j-1)*Astar+Q(1,2)+(j-2)*Astar)==0

# Canonical first-transform eight-state local coefficient table.
states=[]
for parity in (0,1):
    for a1 in (0,1):
        for a2 in (0,1):
            t=(a1-a2+3*parity)%6
            x1=(4*a1+(t!=0)*(1+t)+parity*(a1+a2))%6
            x2=(4*a2+(t!=0)*(1-t)+parity*(a1+a2))%6
            target=4 if parity or (a1==a2==1) else 0
            assert x1==x2==target
            states.append((parity,a1,a2,t,x1))
# Full inverse canonical ledger: lengths, not a proof of the norm bound.
r,l,V,M,Ai,B,t,g,th,v,j,dk,R=s.symbols(
    "r l V M Ai B t g th v j dk R")
F=r+3*l+V
si=r-Ai-B-t
H=2*r-2*B-M+4*l+2*V+dk-j
kappa=M-2*r-2*l-V-dk+Ai+B-R/2
lam=H-th-(si-g)
C=l+R/2-j+t+g-th
D=l+Ai+t+g-th+V
Nc=r-Ai-B-t-g-v
Vc=B+th+v+j
Fc=Nc+Vc
Mc=2*(si-g)-H+dk+2*th
checks=[Fc-(F-D-2*l+j),Mc-(M-2*D-2*l+j),
        Fc-Mc-(F-M+D),
        4*Fc-3*Mc-(4*F-3*M+2*(Ai+t+g-th+V)+j),
        kappa+lam+C+2*Fc-F,
        kappa+H+si+B+t+l+R/2-(F-B-j)]
assert all(s.expand(c)==0 for c in checks)
# Initialization ledger.
rr,z,G,P,m=s.symbols("rr z G P m")
Dp=rr+z-2*G
Finit=Dp-P
Minit=2*Dp-m-2*P
kinit=m-2*Dp+P
assert s.expand(Finit-Minit-(P+G)-(z-G)-(m-rr-2*z+2*G))==0
assert s.expand(4*Finit-3*Minit-6*(z-G)
                -(3*m-2*rr-8*z+10*G+2*P))==0
assert s.expand(kinit+P+2*Finit-m)==0
# Extended plain endpoint, including equality at kappa = 13/18.
kap=Q(13,18)
assert 6*kap-1==Q(10,3)
assert 1/(6*(6*kap-1))==Q(1,20)
assert 1/(6*kap)==Q(3,13)

# Quotient-free Euler identities as rational function identities.
V,W,D,E,A,U,v = s.symbols("V W D E A U v")
Pstar = E-D
Praw = 1/(1-V)+W/(1-W)+Pstar
H = Praw*(1-V)*(1-W)/(1-D)
G = ((A-U)*(1-V)*(1-W)*Pstar-U*(1-V*W))/(1-D)
defect = (D*(V+W-V*W)-V*W+(1-V)*(1-W)*E)/(1-D)
assert s.cancel(H-1-defect) == 0
assert s.cancel((A*Pstar-U*Praw)*(1-V)*(1-W)/(1-D)-G) == 0
# At an unramified selected prime, A=v^(-1)/D and U=W/v.
G0 = G.subs({A:1/(v*D),U:W/v})
offid = (1-V)*(1-W)/(1-D)*(
    (1/v-W/v)*(V/(1-V)-D)+(1/(v*D)+1/v-W/v)*E)
assert s.cancel(G0+H/v-offid) == 0
principalid = (1-W)*(H-1+(V-D)/(1-D))
principalid += (1-V)*(1-W)*E/(D*(1-D))
assert s.cancel(G0.subs(v,1)+H-principalid) == 0
# At a row prime, W=D=0; A and U remain the physical monomials.
assert s.cancel(G.subs({W:0,D:0})-
    (A*(1-V)*E-U*H.subs({W:0,D:0}))) == 0
print("Five quotient-free Euler rational identities passed.")

print("All exact local and transformation assertions passed.")
print("off-row maxima =", offmax)
print("ramified maxima =", rammax)
print("eight states =", states)
\end{verbatim}



\section{Coefficient transport in the two moment inductions}
The canonical recursion must close in a coefficient class, not just in
its affine length invariants.  The following identities make that
closure explicit.  They are also useful when considering any further
change of moment capacities.

\subsection{The first inverse transform with no implicit outer factor}
Use the inverse-appendix notation \(n_i=c_i u_i\),
\(c_i=\mathfrak A_iC\), \(d=d_k\), and
\(\xi_R=\prod_{p\mid R_1}\chi_p^{t_p}\).
Let \(\Gamma_R\) be its normalized primitive Gauss root, with
\(\xi_R=\Gamma_R=1\) for \(R_1=1\).  For
\(a(c)=\bar\alpha(c)\gamma_2(c)\nu(c)\rho(c)\), the complete
outer arithmetic coefficient is
\[
\begin{split}
 {\cal C}^{\rm out}_1={}&w(f)\beta(b_1,f)\overline{\beta(b_2,f)}
      a(c_1)\overline{a(c_2)}
      \chi_{c_1}(f)^4\overline{\chi_{c_2}(f)^4}\\
 &\times\Gamma_R\xi_R(d)\overline{\xi_R(h)} .
\end{split}
\]
It excludes only the divisor scalar \(\mu(d)\), positive norm
denominators, marks and smooth weights.  None of its factors is
defined by division.

CRT for the three coprime primitive moduli gives
\[
\begin{split}
 \gamma(\psi_1;m_1)
 ={}&\gamma_1(u_1)\gamma_{-1}(u_2)\Gamma_R{\cal R}(u_1,u_2)\\
 &\times\chi_{u_1}(R_1)\overline{\chi_{u_2}(R_1)}
                     \xi_R(u_1)\xi_R(u_2).
\end{split}
\]
Combining this with
\(a(cu)=a(c)a(u)\chi_u(c)^4\) and the Gauss signal converts the
entire raw arithmetic factor into
\[
 {\cal C}^{\rm out}_1\,
  G([u_1][u_2]^{-1}){\cal R}([u_1][u_2]^{-1},P_T)
  {\cal K}(u_1)\overline{{\cal K}(u_2)},
\]
where
\[
 {\cal K}(u)=\mu(u)\nu(u)\rho(u)
       \overline{\chi_u(hf^2E)}\chi_u(C)^4\chi_u(d)\xi(u).
\]
The eight local states determine the common \(\xi\).
Fourier expansion of the one quotient-class function gives
\(\nu_1=\nu_2=\nu\vartheta\); its coefficient stays outer.
This is stronger bookkeeping than allowing unspecified ray phases
at intermediate steps.  The statement and proof are in the answer's
equations \texttt{eq:inverse-first-outer-explicit} and
\texttt{eq:inverse-first-arithmetic-identity}.

The extension off the mutually coprime residual support is defined
by this quotient-free product and the formal product norms.
It is not a Poisson formula for a newly enlarged conductor.
The complete M\"obius indicator is inserted before any factorwise
estimate and recovers the genuine domain exactly.

\subsection{New-label zeros and the positive measures}
After the second inverse transform,
\[
 k_{\rm new}=d_kd_2k'',\qquad f_{\rm new}=JCd_2v',\qquad
 \rho'=\rho\,1_{(\,\cdot\,,\operatorname{rad}q_0t'(g'/d_2))=1}.
\]
The exact character identity is
\[
 \chi_n(d_k)\chi_n(k'')\overline{\chi_n(d_2)}
       \chi_n(CJ)^4\chi_n(v')^4
 =\chi_n(k_{\rm new})\chi_n(f_{\rm new})^4 .
\]
It holds on nonunits as well.  Squarefreeness of \(f_{\rm new}\)
uses \((C,J)=1\), \(g'\) squarefree,
\((g',CJ)=1\), \(d_2\mid g'\), and
\((v',g'CJ)=1\).  The common magnitudes \(|B_o(g')|^2\) and
\(|D_o(v';d_2,k'')|^2\) retain these zeros until the weighted
Cauchy inequality.  Dropping them earlier would not justify a
squarefree new-label average.

The fixed triples \((q_0,t',g'/d_2)\) range over their
\emph{source projection}, not the larger product of norm balls
used solely for counting.  A source witness supplies
\(\log_Zq_{q_0}\le\ell+\eta\), which is needed for the
new puncture invariant.  The child label stays averaged.
The fibre bound splits into a fixed-triple divisor factor and
\(d_{\mathcal O}(f_{\rm new})^{9+4K_{\rm slot}}\);
it has no extra row count.  The old \(f\) was removed by the
divisibility fibre \(f^2E\mid(hf^2E)\).
The initialization has the analogous fixed quotient \(t=C/d'\),
new label \(d's\), and row \(d'h'\).
These facts explain why restoring each column normalization and
invoking the child exponent costs \(2F_{\rm child}\), while each
outer family and each Fourier density is counted only once.

\subsection{Why the centered plain coefficient survives}
The smooth kernel and both inverse roots depend on the
\emph{whole} column products.  On a fixed logarithmic box, one
common Fourier inversion gives modes
\[
 (q_a/T_a)^{it_a},\qquad (q_b/T_b)^{it_b}.
\]
The second is implemented by the negative mode inside the conjugated
polynomial.  Since \(a=\Pi l_1l_2\), a side acquires
\(q_\Pi^{it_a}q_{l_1}^{it_a}q_{l_2}^{it_a}\); it never acquires
two independently chosen exponents on its plain variables.
This is the content of the answer's
\texttt{lem:whole-product-transport}.

Genuine complete extraction of fixed exact valuations punctures all
residual factors at the common support.  By contrast the artificial
divisor allocation
\[
 1_{p\mid\prod n_i}=1-\prod_i(1-1_{p\mid n_i})
\]
replaces a selected plain variable by \(pl_i\) with \emph{no}
condition \(p\nmid l_i\).  Both its rectangle scales are divided
by the same exact \(q_p\).  Repeating these operations preserves
the equality of their products and the common character and mask.
The Gauss correlation removes the Gauss factors before any live
slot is summed absolutely.  Only on an exceptional child row is
the resulting fixed-character lattice main term canceled.

Invalid alternatives to avoid: a column-dependent Fourier density;
a separate absolute bound for each term of the centered difference
before that cancellation; a quotient puncture after artificial
divisor extraction; or replacing the source projection by its
containing norm balls when checking child hypotheses.
None is needed for the written proof.

\subsection{Witness orientation and physical slots}
A positive-orientation inverse/plain pair can be conjugated together;
its absolute lower bounds, lengths, and common presentation are
preserved, and \(\Theta\) is conjugation-closed.  For
\(\psi_u(n)=\nu(n)\overline{\chi_n(u)}\) and \(p\notin S\),
\[
 \psi_u(p)\overline{\nu(p)}1_{p\in1_T}
       =\overline{\chi_p(u)}1_{p\in1_T}.
\]
Thus the prime coefficient for the marked moment is
\(\overline\nu\,1_{1_T}\).  Conjugating the whole selected product
for the plain moment gives \(\nu\,1_{1_T}\).
Both have finite character expansions in \(\Theta\), with
coefficients independent of the row.  There is no lower bound for
a zero-amplitude slot, and no row-dependent reweighting.

\section{Additional exact coefficient certificate}
The following script is
\texttt{moment\_transport\_certificate.py}.  Its finite ray group
is \(C_3\times C_2\times C_2\), with phases in twelfth roots of
unity; this simultaneously represents the cubic supplement and
the quadratic-refinement table.  Character zeros are represented
by a separate absorbing value, including in zeroth powers.
Thus the \(7^6\) moving-character tests exhaust that elementary
algebra, rather than sample field primes.  The all-modulus proof
still uses CRT and the signal identity as written above; this
script does not prove analytic moment bounds.
\begin{verbatim}
"""Exact coefficient-transport checks for the written moment proofs.
Python 3 + SymPy; no floats, sampling, network, or analytic certification.
The finite ray identities use the proved quadratic-refinement table.
"""
from itertools import product
from collections import Counter
import sympy as s

counts = Counter()

# Fixed ray data for G: a cubic character times the four square classes.
# Phases are exponents of zeta_12. R is the bicharacter from the proof.
ray = list(product(range(3), range(2), range(2)))

def add(a, b):
    return ((a[0]+b[0]) % 3, (a[1]+b[1]) % 2, (a[2]+b[2]) % 2)

def neg(a):
    return ((-a[0]) % 3, a[1], a[2])

def sub(a, b):
    return add(a, neg(b))

def G(a):
    k, e, f = a
    return (-4*k + 3*f - 6*e*f) % 12

def R(a, b):
    _, e, f = a
    _, g, h = b
    return (6*(e*h + f*g + f*h)) % 12

for a, b in product(ray, repeat=2):
    assert (G(add(a,b))-G(a)-G(b)-R(a,b)) % 12 == 0
    assert (G(neg(a))-R(a,a)+G(a)) % 12 == 0
    # Exact second-Poisson signal phase.
    raw = R(a,a)-G(a)+G(b)+R(a,b)
    assert (raw-G(sub(b,a))) % 12 == 0
    counts["ray_refinement_pairs"] += 1

for a, b, p in product(ray, repeat=3):
    # Exact first-Poisson ray factor, before finite Fourier expansion.
    raw = G(a)+R(b,b)-G(b)+R(a,b)+R(a,p)+R(b,p)
    target = G(sub(a,b))+R(sub(a,b),p)
    assert (raw-target) % 12 == 0
    # Common extracted ideal cancels from the second ray quotient.
    assert G(sub(add(p,b),add(p,a))) == G(sub(b,a))
    counts["three_ray_class_checks"] += 1

# Zero-extended sixth roots. None means zero, integers are zeta_6 powers.
# Even the zeroth power of zero is zero in these character conventions.
roots = [None] + list(range(6))

def mul(*values):
    return None if any(v is None for v in values) else sum(values) % 6

def zp(v, k):
    return None if v is None else (k*v) % 6

def conj(v):
    return zp(v, -1)

for z in roots:
    assert conj(z) == mul(z, zp(z, 4))
    assert zp(z, 4) == conj(zp(z, 2))
    counts["zero_extended_single_factor"] += 1

for parity, a1, a2 in product((0,1), repeat=3):
    t = (a1-a2+3*parity) % 6
    E = parity*(a1+a2)
    target = 4 if parity or a1*a2 else 0
    for z in roots:
        # The zeroth power retains the residual puncture at this prime.
        for ai, sign in ((a1,1), (a2,-1)):
            raw = zp(z, 4*ai + (t != 0)*(1+sign*t))
            moved = mul(conj(zp(z,E)), zp(z,target))
            assert raw == moved
        counts["first_transform_masked_states"] += 1

for dk, kpp, d2, C, J, v in product(roots, repeat=6):
    old = mul(dk, kpp, conj(d2), zp(C,4), zp(J,4), zp(v,4))
    new = mul(mul(dk,d2,kpp), zp(mul(J,C,d2,v),4))
    assert old == new
    counts["second_transform_all_zero_patterns"] += 1

# One-prime support check, not a random test of ideals.
# g'=d2*r_g is squarefree; B_o and D_o are retained through Cauchy.
for J,C,d2,rg,v,q0,t,rho,dk,kpp in product((0,1), repeat=10):
    if J*C or d2*rg or (d2+rg)*(J+C) or v*(J+C+d2+rg):
        continue
    fnew_exp = J+C+d2+v
    assert fnew_exp <= 1
    old_support = bool(J or C or q0 or t or rho
                       or dk or d2 or rg or v or kpp)
    row_support = bool(dk or d2 or kpp)
    label_support = bool(fnew_exp)
    puncture_support = bool(rho or q0 or t or rg)
    assert old_support == (row_support or label_support or puncture_support)
    counts["canonical_child_support_patterns"] += 1

# Full inclusion-exclusion does not add a quotient puncture.
for mask in product((0,1), repeat=4):
    rhs = 0
    for chosen in product((0,1), repeat=4):
        size = sum(chosen)
        if size:
            term = (-1)**(size+1)
            for b, keep in zip(mask, chosen):
                if keep:
                    term *= b
            rhs += term
    assert rhs == int(any(mask))
    counts["full_factor_allocation_patterns"] += 1

# Common product scales after arbitrary extracted factors.
X1,X2,Y1,d1,d2 = s.symbols("X1 X2 Y1 d1 d2", positive=True)
Y2 = X1*X2/Y1
assert s.cancel((X1/d1)*(X2/d2)-(Y1/d1)*(Y2/d2)) == 0
# A single Fourier mode supplies the same exponent to both plain factors.
x1,x2,pi = s.symbols("x1 x2 pi", real=True)
assert s.expand((x1+x2+pi)-(x1+x2)-pi) == 0

# Boundary accounting for separate clipped rectangles (an identity).
r1,r2,e1,e2,p01,p02,p1,p2,w1,w2,t0 = s.symbols(
    "r1 r2 e1 e2 p01 p02 p1 p2 w1 w2 t0")
rc1 = r1-(p1-p01)
rc2 = r2-(p2-p02)
lhs = t0-(rc1+rc2)/2+(e1+p01+e2+p02)/2
rhs = t0-(r1+r2)/2+(e1+p1+e2+p2)/2
assert s.expand(lhs-rhs) == 0

print("All exact coefficient-transport assertions passed.")
for name in sorted(counts):
    print(name, "=", counts[name])
print("Scope: algebra and support identities only; not an L-function proof.")
\end{verbatim}
Its output is
\begin{verbatim}
All exact coefficient-transport assertions passed.
canonical_child_support_patterns = 192
first_transform_masked_states = 56
full_factor_allocation_patterns = 16
ray_refinement_pairs = 144
second_transform_all_zero_patterns = 117649
three_ray_class_checks = 1728
zero_extended_single_factor = 7
Scope: algebra and support identities only; not an L-function proof.
\end{verbatim}
The normalized UTF-8 digest, with one terminal newline, is
\nolinkurl{06f0b04e5c41c2451de6b3ff5822ca3d30fb1f34b319de3b7efb2119defd5c1f}.

\section{Version-pinned additional literature comparison}
Liu, arXiv:2610.12234v1, submitted 8 October 2026, Theorem 21.1,
states the all-family boundary
\((1507-2\sqrt{921})/1653\).
The version-specific abstract and PDF were inspected on
9 October 2026; no byte hash or proof validation is inferred.
This equals the fixed-\(\kappa_0=3/4\) value already computed above.
Our rational comparison is elementary: for
\(g_-=5037/10^{11}\), \(g_+=5038/10^{11}\), the quantities
\(T_\pm=1507-1653(\theta+g_\pm)\) are positive, and
\[
 T_-^2-3684=\frac{1639146362533017529}{10^{24}}>0,\qquad
 T_+^2-3684=-\frac{367462194982611191}{10^{24}}<0.
\]
Hence \(g_-<B_{\rm Liu}-\theta<g_+\).
This literature comparison is not an input to the continuation
proof or either recursive moment.

and no extracted text span lies outside its physical page rectangle.
The class is exactly the required 12-point article, and a static

\subsection{Fallback when only the notes are mounted}
The raw notes file carries a base64-encoded, deterministic ZIP source
capsule \emph{after} its first \verb|\end{document}|.  This keeps
the encoded payload out of a typeset notes document.
It contains the following exact current sources:
\begin{verbatim}
answer.tex
references.bib
exact_certificate.py
local_transform_certificate.py
finite_local_certificate.py
moment_transport_certificate.py
verify_submission.py
source_capsule_manifest.json
\end{verbatim}
It intentionally contains neither this notes file (avoiding
self-reference) nor a substitute proof.  The stored answer is the
entire standalone canonical document, including both long moment
appendices.  The capsule's SHA256 is
\nolinkurl{af92b41541e01655553ea1e4e6d276bd93af170bf80d4af2b3116807edb4a5fc}.
The manifest authenticates each member; it is not a mathematical
certificate.  For a source-only reproduction, extract the named
members with the following code, then run
\texttt{python /mnt/data/verify\_submission.py}.
This driver checks source hashes, runs all four exact scripts,
builds the whole answer twice, checks its metadata and formatting,
and records the actual PDF page count and final compiler log.
It uses Python with SymPy, \texttt{pdflatex}, and \texttt{pdfinfo};
PyMuPDF adds an optional page-boundary check.  No old reference
archive or outbound network is required.

\begin{verbatim}
"""Extract the version-pinned source capsule from a mounted notes file.
Usage: python extract_source_capsule.py /path/to/research_notes.tex
Outputs use canonical filenames in /mnt/data; no network is used.
"""
from pathlib import Path
from io import BytesIO
import base64, hashlib, json, re, sys, zipfile
notes = Path(sys.argv[1]).read_text()
pattern = (r"^% QRH_SOURCE_CAPSULE_BEGIN\n(.*?)"
           r"^% QRH_SOURCE_CAPSULE_END$")
match = re.search(pattern, notes, re.M | re.S)
assert match, "This notes file has no source capsule."
encoded = "".join(line[2:] for line in match.group(1).splitlines()
                  if line.startswith("% "))
raw = base64.b64decode(encoded, validate=True)
assert hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest() == "af92b41541e01655553ea1e4e6d276bd93af170bf80d4af2b3116807edb4a5fc"
expected = {
 "answer.tex", "references.bib", "exact_certificate.py",
 "local_transform_certificate.py", "finite_local_certificate.py",
 "moment_transport_certificate.py", "verify_submission.py",
 "source_capsule_manifest.json",
}
with zipfile.ZipFile(BytesIO(raw)) as archive:
    names = archive.namelist()
    print("Capsule members:", names)
    assert set(names) == expected and len(names) == len(expected)
    manifest = json.loads(archive.read("source_capsule_manifest.json"))
    assert set(manifest["files"]) == expected-{"source_capsule_manifest.json"}
    for name in names:
        data = archive.read(name)
        if name != "source_capsule_manifest.json":
            record = manifest["files"][name]
            assert len(data) == record["bytes"]
            assert hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest() == record["sha256"]
        Path("/mnt/data", name).write_bytes(data)
print("Exact complete sources restored; then run:")
print("python /mnt/data/verify_submission.py")
\end{verbatim}
The extractor first prints and checks the member list.  It writes
only the whitelisted names, verifies every member hash, and does not
unpack an arbitrary archive wholesale.  Its execution was tested by
decoding this payload, checking equality with every current source,
and running the full driver on the restored \texttt{answer.tex}.
The decoded proof source is byte-identical to the canonical one.
The logs and \texttt{build\_receipt.json} record artifact and finite
algebra checks, not an independent proof review.

\end{document}

% Operator-provided historical review context (not proof authority):

# Referee report on the revised draft

## 1. Overall assessment

**The revision addresses the mathematical objections in my previous report. I find no remaining essential gap in the revised proof, and assess it as solving the primary all-Dirichlet target.**

The constant and improvement are
\[
\theta=\frac{3499999}{4000000}=0.87499975,
\qquad
\frac78-\theta=\frac1{4000000}>0.
\]

This assessment does **not** rest on accepting the baseline’s zero-free theorem. The important change is that Appendices A–E now supply arguments for the previously assumed arithmetic package, including the full-frequency reflection, the two masked norm estimates, and the two distinct moment arguments.

The document also passes the **current 200-page LaTeX contract**: I reproduced a clean compilation to **48 pages**. The former 16-page limit is superseded by the revised contract.

This remains a mathematical referee assessment, not a formal verification or a substitute for the independent human review contemplated by the problem.

---

## 2. Changes relative to the previous review

| Previous concern | Assessment of the revision |
|---|---|
| Essential arithmetic hypothesis \(\mathsf A\) remained unproved | **Addressed substantively.** Appendices B–E provide the missing arguments rather than merely relabeling the assumptions. |
| Completed reflection, including nonunit frequencies and uniform profiles, was unresolved | **Addressed.** Appendix B derives the required transformation from the specified theta formulas and treats the previously problematic branches explicitly. |
| Principal tuple bound incorrectly appeared uniform as \(\Re z\to+\infty\) | **Corrected.** The bound is now on each fixed bounded real box \(\mathcal K\), with the appropriate dependence on \(\mathcal K\). |
| Fixed \(O(e)\) conductor losses were mixed with freely prescribable losses | **Corrected.** The manuscript separates \(12e\) from \(\epsilon_c'\) and carries the resulting \(12de\) into the exponent calculation. |
| Greedy slot selection needed its ordering specified | **Corrected.** Slots are ordered by decreasing \(g_i\), and the fractional-selection argument is supplied. |
| Fourier inversion needed the weighted inverse identified | **Corrected.** The proof explicitly uses \(e^{-C(2)u}f_\eta(e^u)\). |
| Primitive induction of the norm lift should be explicit | **Corrected.** The deleted-factor equality for the inducing primitive Hecke character is recorded. |
| Independent verification of the author’s source hashes and audit bundle was unavailable | **Still a review limitation, not a mathematical objection.** See below. |

I found **no new blocking mathematical error** in the added appendices or the altered main argument.

---

## 3. Materials, verification limits, and compilation

### 3.1 What was reviewed

I re-read the full revised inline manuscript, including all appendices and references. I did not assume that arguments accepted provisionally in the previous review remained valid.

The current research-notes snapshot was available. The initial mandatory checker verified:

- **135,302 bytes**
- SHA-256  
  `6d7d8b07bb78a34a9b01fefa743181a9992c14349da824d30196dc5e2fe2a42d`

Only targeted background passages were consulted. No argument appearing solely in the notes has been counted as part of the proof.

The requested final checker was submitted again as my last tool call, but the tool refused execution because its call limit had been reached. I therefore **do not claim that the final rerun succeeded**. The successful initial checker wrote its receipt, and neither the notes nor that receipt was subsequently modified by my review work.

### 3.2 Source access

The baseline TeX was readable through the web tool at the exact specified revision. I inspected relevant dependency passages, including the local-error estimate, canonical coefficient extraction, child normalization, and support-window conventions. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/math/fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb/preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-September-30-2026/build/paper.tex))

Direct requests for the three pinned PDFs failed. No Compute worker was exposed to this reviewer. Consequently, I have **not independently reproduced the author’s four-file byte/hash authentication**.

The linked author audit ZIP and original `answer.tex`/PDF were not attached in the accessible filesystem. I recreated the full inline document for compilation and made independent checks. The author’s assertion that “63 checks passed” is therefore not presented here as something I independently reran.

These are provenance and review-access limitations. They do not leave a mathematical step dependent on unavailable research notes or an unavailable numerical certificate: the essential arguments and continuous certificate are in `answer.tex`.

---

## 4. Literature validation

The imported results were checked for their relevant hypotheses, not merely for the existence of the cited papers.

- **Dunn–Radziwiłł:** The cited theta automorphy, infinity coefficients, and cusp expansions are the appropriate unconditional inputs. The three coefficient families used in Appendix B agree with the source, including the conjugation and sign change in the Fourier index. The manuscript does not import the paper’s GRH-conditional prime conclusions. ([arxiv.org](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2109.07463v3))
- **Goldmakher–Louvel:** Definition 1 requires a primitive quadratic Hecke family with reciprocity through a fixed finite group and the specified primitive-product property. Theorem 1.1 then supplies the squarefree quadratic large sieve used in (C1). Appendix C checks these family conditions rather than omitting them. ([arxiv.org](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1112.1642v2))
- **Heath-Brown:** Theorem 2 has precisely the \(N+L+(NL)^{2/3}\) bound, with both variables squarefree Eisenstein integers congruent to \(1\pmod3\). It does not impose squarefree rational norms or prohibit rational prime factors. The Oxford download failed, but the original-paper text was accessible elsewhere and its theorem statement was inspected. ([researchgate.net](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/254847691_Kummer%27s_conjecture_for_cubic_Gauss_sums))
- **Thorner–Zaman:** The cited result includes the possible exceptional-zero contribution. Its fixed-extension consequence justifies the eventual prime asymptotic used here; it does not require a conductor-uniform starting scale. ([arxiv.org](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1803.02823))
- **Poonen:** Theorem 5.22(b)–(c) supplies the Hecke continuation and functional equation used in the manuscript. ([math.mit.edu](https://math.mit.edu/~poonen/786/notes.pdf))
- **DLMF:** Equation 25.15.4 supports the primitive/imprimitive Dirichlet factorization. ([dlmf.nist.gov](https://dlmf.nist.gov/25.15))
- **Milne:** VIII.5.3 and VIII.5.5 supply the power-residue Artin-map identity and factorization through a ray class group required for the fixed numerator families. ([jmilne.org](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf))

The exploration reference is appropriately identified as heuristic work at another revision, rather than as a proved improvement. The manuscript’s qualified priority statement is acceptable; this review does not certify exhaustive literature coverage. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tomoto0/quasi-riemann-hypothesis-7-8-verification/main/reports/improvement_exploration.md))

---

# 5. Main-text audit

## Section 1: Result and source dependence

### Constant and scope

The numerical constant satisfies the required range, and the improvement is exactly the stated positive rational number. The strict/non-strict distinction is consistently maintained.

### Arithmetic-input paragraphs

The crucial difference from the earlier draft is that the arithmetic package now has written proofs and dependency checks in the submission. It is no longer an explicit unresolved hypothesis.

The remaining premise in the positive-slot plain moment,
\[
B\le \frac{1+\kappa}{2}\qquad(\kappa<1),
\]
does not make the final theorem conditional: the later definition of \(\kappa\) ensures it. At \(\kappa=1\), the argument uses the unconditional absolute prime bound.

The dependency structure is acyclic. In particular, the plain fourth moment is not derived from the inverse moment, and neither arithmetic argument imports the desired zero-free conclusion.

### Source-authentication and attribution paragraphs

The manuscript correctly separates authentication from mathematical validation. The authentication assertions remain author-reported rather than independently rehashed in this review.

The novelty paragraph identifies the new work without claiming priority for parameter variation itself.

---

## Section 2: Geometry, continuation, and transfer

### Geometry

The identities
\[
l_y-l_x=\frac18,\qquad l_x+l_y=1-\ell,\qquad h=1-l_x+\ell
\]
and
\[
C(s)=s-\frac{11}{16},\qquad
C(\theta_\lambda)=\frac{l_x}{2}+\frac b{12}
\]
check exactly.

Thus the deformation does not inadvertently change the Mellin signal being compared.

### Family supremum

The definition of \(B\) includes real zeros and all heights and excludes poles. The proof uses only \(B\le1\) initially. There is no hidden bootstrap from the baseline’s \(11/12\) or \(7/8\) theorem.

Passing to primitive inducing characters and interpreting norm twists as vertical translations are consistent with the family used.

### Continuation criterion

The proof is valid.

The two estimates yield the required growth of \(f_\eta\) at infinity. Rightward contour shifts yield sufficiently rapid decay at zero. The explicitly identified weighted function
\[
u\mapsto e^{-C(2)u}f_\eta(e^u)
\]
has the integrability needed for Fourier inversion.

The Mellin transform is holomorphic on the asserted half-plane. Its product with the meromorphic \(L\)-function equals a nowhere-zero holomorphic function. This excludes zeros without treating a principal pole as a zero or assuming the supremum is attained.

The common positive exponent saving, rather than a common starting threshold, is what this argument needs.

### Imprimitive and principal Dirichlet characters

The finite Euler factors are holomorphic units for \(\Re s>0\). The principal residue and the empty product for \(q=1\) are correctly handled.

### Norm lift and cancellation at one

The added primitive-induction paragraph removes the previous exposition omission. The split/inert factorization is correct, and the possible principal-companion cancellation at \(s=1\) is explicitly addressed using
\[
L(1,\varepsilon_3)
=\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{(3n+1)(3n+2)}>0.
\]

The transfer therefore preserves the required strict half-plane and includes the exceptional real cases.

---

## Section 3: Changed low estimate

### Physical probe

The proof continues to use the actual modified physical probe and its whole-index marks. It does not substitute a separately defined high-side expression.

The disjointness of the underlying prime windows is retained through the relevant coefficient constructions.

### Elementary energy inequality

The proof checks. In particular, the two branches for the minimum and the signed slack terms are handled with sufficient constants.

I independently verified **exact rational nonnegative-multiplier certificates for all 24 affine branches** of the inequality. These certificates cover the continuous and unbounded variable ranges in the lemma; they are not numerical sampling.

### Scale conditions

The inequalities ensuring eventual admissibility of \(Q,Y',P_a\), and
\[
P_a^2/Y'\ll P_a^{1/6},
\]
hold throughout the stated deformation range.

### Reflected-energy application

The joint smooth separation is performed before evaluating rows and live slots. The subsequent slot coefficients retain the required row independence.

The correspondence between the energy variables and the source variables is explicit. The new Appendix C supplies the general estimate needed here, not merely its old specialized low-side consequence.

### Final exponent

After Cauchy–Schwarz, rescaling, and tuple counting, the excess
\[
-d+\frac{(5\ell+d-1)_+}{8}
\]
is correctly bounded by \(-7d/8\), since \(\ell\le1/5\).

I found no missing tuple-count factor or duplicated powerful-part count.

---

## Section 4: Principal local factors and comparison

### Real-box correction

The previous overstatement has been repaired correctly. The dependence on
\[
\sup_{y\in\operatorname{supp}W_i}y^{\Re z-1}
\]
is now acknowledged. All actual contour applications use fixed real boxes, so this repair suffices.

### Local identities and contraction

The displayed rational cancellation identities check algebraically. The decay exponents on (P), the ideal-counting majorant, and the numerical contraction are consistent:
\[
336\cdot\frac{225}{4}(10^{10})^{-4/5}
=\frac{189}{10^6}.
\]

The larger open region supplies normal convergence around the closed working region.

### Prime normalizer

The fixed-extension prime asymptotic establishes eventual nonvanishing of every selected sum. Nonnegative weights are used in the relative-error argument, rather than being an unused assumption.

A possible exceptional zero changes the starting scale but does not invalidate the fixed-extension asymptotic. No unjustified moving-conductor estimate is used.

### Principal contour calculation

The three error exponents check exactly. The proof crosses only the stated numerator poles, estimates the residue error on the line where it is controlled, and shifts only the main residue term to \(\Re s=2\).

The treatment includes \(B=1\) and principal targets. The pole at one produces a reciprocal zero, not an obstructing singularity.

---

## Section 5: Row envelopes

### Choice of \(\kappa\)

The choice
\[
\kappa=\max(3/4,2B-1)
\]
satisfies the plain-moment premise throughout \(B\le1\). The endpoint \(\kappa=1\) is treated directly.

### Detector and floor

The floor is correctly distinguished from bins with a witnessing zero. The truncated-inverse error decays under the stated restriction on \(T_1\).

No nonzero-imaginary-part assumption is introduced.

### Greedy selection

The new descending-amplitude ordering supplies the previously missing justification. The fractional initial segment has sufficient weighted amplitude, and removing its last fractional slot loses at most
\[
\frac{\delta}{2}\max w_i
\]
before squaring.

### Moment compatibility

The proof preserves:

- individual row-independent prime coefficients;
- whole-product conjugation for the plain moment;
- original zero extensions;
- the inducing-character exclusion;
- separate treatment of bounded exceptional physical rows.

The Sobolev argument is applied to smooth witness parameters, not to arbitrary row-dependent arithmetic coefficients.

### Capacity optimization

The affine crossing formulas, \(R_*\), and the capacity correction check. The inverse capacity stays within its strict admissibility margins, and the required supply is at most \(7/37\).

The endpoints near \(r=1\) and zero plain capacity are handled with unselected estimates, not by taking a limit of constants from a strict marked estimate.

The \(\delta\ge5/6\) envelope and the no-slot \(t=1\) envelope are consistent with their respective witnesses.

---

## Section 6: Nonprincipal comparison

### Contours and holomorphy

The proposed contours satisfy the stated inequalities for \({\cal D}_1\), including when \(B=1\) and the \(w\)-line is slightly negative.

The extension below \(7/8\) is justified by the actual working region; it is not inferred merely from an improved endpoint certificate.

### Tails and horizontal joins

The proof respects the distinction between global and buffered estimates. It moves the original bin sum before subdividing the retained integrand into amplitude pieces.

The independent decay coordinates and the order of internal versus external decay choices are sufficient for the asserted tails.

### Local errors and the \(e\)-loss

The revision now records the fixed loss correctly:
\[
U^{R+\delta/2+12e+\epsilon_c'}.
\]
The resulting real loss is
\[
(16-6l_y+12d)e+(1+d)\epsilon_c'
+d\epsilon_d+\epsilon_p.
\]

The source’s proof explicitly has the \(12e\) conductor/deletion bound, with the strict selected factors paid by distinct factors of one simultaneous conductor deficit. This confirms that the revision is not merely replacing an unspecified \(O(e)\) by an unsupported numerical constant. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/math/fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb/preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-September-30-2026/build/paper.tex))

The coefficient of \(e\) remains bounded on the intermediate row range, so it can be absorbed by the later common choice of \(e\).

### Small rows

The small-row exponent checks. Bounded exceptional rows, nonidentity unit rows, and potentially principal denominators are included.

### Large rows

The displayed infinite-tail exponent is less than \(-1\) throughout the specified parameter range.

The very large fixed \(z_\infty\) changes constants and required smooth orders, but does not invalidate the contour argument or introduce an unbounded parameter into the later order of choices.

---

## Section 7: Endpoint certificate and closure

### Continuous certificate

The polynomial identity expands exactly. Its sign argument covers the whole stated rectangle in \((\delta,x)\), not a grid.

The perturbation identity also checks, and
\[
m_*=\frac{59873}{550800000},
\qquad
m_*-2\cdot10^{-6}>\frac1{10000}.
\]

### Other endpoints

The table entries, the middle-row expression, and the relevant slopes check. The endpoint reductions are justified by those slope signs.

### Comparison with \(C(B)\)

The subtraction of \(B-\theta_\lambda\) is performed correctly. The adverse \(\Delta_+\)-dependent capacity term is absorbed without assuming a preliminary zero-free theorem.

The row-range extension and supply inequality cover the remaining intermediate interval.

### Order of choices and uniformity

The choices are arranged in a permissible order:

1. Common real margins and moment losses.
2. Mesh and fixed slot system.
3. Remaining real losses, including \(e\).
4. Target-dependent internal height and scale orders.
5. \(T_1=Z^\tau\).
6. External decay order \(N\).

The common high saving survives the target-dependent choice of \(\tau\). Neither \(J_\eta\) nor \(f_\eta\) is redefined using these auxiliary tail parameters.

It is correct that \(\lambda/4<\mu\) is unnecessary. The continuation criterion only needs a common positive high saving, however small.

---

# 6. Audit of the new arithmetic appendices

## Appendix A: Conventions and smooth dependence

### Finite arithmetic identities

The primary-generator convention, additive pairing, zero extensions, and fixed-data distinctions are suitable for the later transformations.

The Gauss–Jacobi identities are applied in cases where the relevant characters and products are nonprincipal. The supplementary phase is fixed rather than left as an unspecified unit.

The treatment of fixed \(S\)-supported numerators through a finite ray family is legitimate. Moving good primes are not absorbed into that fixed family.

### Smooth separation

The important point is the Euler derivative identity
\[
y_i\partial_{y_i}K(Ym(\mathbf y))
=a_i(x\partial_xK)(Ym(\mathbf y)),
\]
which avoids a derivative-order power of the large scale.

The use of one common Fourier density before evaluating live labels is maintained in the later appendices. The backward choice of smooth and height orders is justified once finite recursion depth has been established.

The Gaussian extension has summable seminorms and does not introduce an uncontrolled \(Z^{J\xi}\) loss.

---

## Appendix B: Completed reflection

This appendix resolves the principal outstanding issue from the previous submission.

### Coefficients and support

The conjugated coefficient is correctly taken as
\[
\overline{d_j(-\mu)}.
\]
The dual support retains shared \(n,b\) primes and the permitted primes above the fixed excluded set. In particular, the ordinary cubic character at the prime of norm \(4\) is not incorrectly replaced by a sextic good-prime character.

The primal whole-index mask and the supplementary-symbol conversion are compatible.

### All finite frequencies

The table (B6) treats both nonunit-frequency possibilities correctly, especially
\[
C_{p,0}(0)=1-q_p^{-1}.
\]

The reduction of each rational cusp is performed before applying the automorphy formula. The three cases according to the \(\lambda_3\)-valuation of the denominator establish the necessary fixed-sector dependence.

The additive CRT phase and the substitution \(h_p\mapsto h_p^{-1}\) produce the claimed \(j_p+2\) exponent, including the \(j_p=4\) Ramanujan branch and the distinct active \(j_p=0\) branch.

### Mellin normalization and continuation

The direct and reflected Bessel integrals are used in their convergence regions. The derivative kills the cusp constants, and the chain rule gives the stated conductor exponent and angular factor.

The entire continuation concerns the angularly weighted product actually used in the proof. It does not incorrectly assert entireness for the unweighted theta series.

The gamma ratio has no pole across the kernel shifts used. Absolute dual convergence is established before the zero-line representation is used.

### Uniform reflected profiles and marks

The kernel saving is extracted from the common profile before taking the row norm, so it is legitimately squared.

The absent/inactive compatibility needed for whole-index marks is established simultaneously, including nonunit fixed frequencies.

Finally, (B16) identifies precisely why the residual-row/active-mark phase cancels and why nonresidual base primes must first be frozen. The resulting coefficient independence is sufficient for Appendix C.

---

## Appendix C: Energy and Gram bounds

### Large-sieve hypotheses

The quadratic family verification supplies the conductor, fixed reciprocity data, and primitive-product condition needed for (C1).

The cubic sieve is used on squarefree Eisenstein indices in the correct orientation and with its \((NL)^{2/3}\) term retained.

### Collision masks and hybrid norm

Both collision masks are expanded fully before the corresponding positive enlargement. The proof does not delete a pair-dependent mask inside an oscillating sum.

After freezing the indicated variables, the coefficients used in the cubic sieve are independent of its moving row. Grouping by the squarefree product has only divisor multiplicity.

### General reflected energy

The coefficient exponent, hybrid exponent, squared kernel saving, and powerful-part count combine to (C8).

The powerful part is counted once. Growing fourth-power labels and punctures remain moving data with controlled divisor multiplicity, rather than being silently added to the fixed arithmetic modulus.

The width inequality (C9) has a valid primewise charge. The raw tail estimate is obtained before retained-range restrictions or positive enlargement, and its crude counting exponent is independent of the decay order.

### Gram estimate

The prime-power Gauss identities include nonunit frequencies. The correlation identity has the stated normalization and diagonal.

The artificial off-coprime extension is used only after the full Möbius identity has been inserted. Mean-free frequencies and sixth-power-supported exceptional frequencies are counted separately.

The resulting terms
\[
1+P_a^{1/6}+P_a^2/Y'
\]
are consistent with the proof and with their later low-side specialization.

---

## Appendix D: Inverse moments

### Canonical class and completion

The restricted canonical coefficient class is stated explicitly. The proof does not claim a bound for arbitrary extra bounded column coefficients.

The masked primitive Poisson identity preserves all zero values. Its principal modulus-one case is included.

The cube completion is inverted with the mark on the whole index; no unjustified condition \((n,b)=1\) is introduced.

### Terminal estimate

The terminal width bound follows from the canonical margins and the shortness of \(V\) and the cube dyad. The three branches in (D5) leave strict room for the allocated losses.

The label average is normalized once, as required.

### Two-transform coefficient extraction

The eight-state table checks. Exponent-zero factors retain their punctures.

The character identity (D8) is valid, including at zeros:
\[
\overline\chi=\chi\,\chi^4.
\]

The new fourth-power label is squarefree on the inherited support. The new puncture is fixed within the child norm and does not depend on its current row or averaged label.

The fibre majorant is independent of the child row. Its use does not count the constituents of \(f'\) a second time.

### Ledger and termination

All identities in (D12) check exactly. In particular, the occurrence of **two copies of \(F_c\)** is correct.

The source-supported projection used for the frozen triples is important and is retained. The row drop and margin deterioration in (D13) give a finite acyclic recursion.

The support convention uses fixed normalized-ratio windows, not annuli whose ratio grows like \(Z^\eta\). The cited source makes this distinction explicitly, including the uniform clipping family. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/math/fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb/preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-September-30-2026/build/paper.tex))

### Initialization and amplification

The overlap normalization and the three identities in (D16) check. The initial puncture and fourth-power label lie in the canonical class with strict margins.

The sixth-power identity (D17) remains valid when \(a\) and \(u\) share primes. The map \((u,a)\mapsto ua^6\) is injective under the stated conventions.

Consequently, the endpoint \(r=1\) is obtained without taking a limit of strict marked-moment constants.

---

## Appendix E: Plain fourth moment

### Larger inductive family

The use of the full displayed moving radical, including canceled exponents, is appropriate. An extra puncture is common to the factors in a row sum; it is not selected separately for each row.

The zero-slot and positive-slot permissible families are distinguished throughout.

### Reflection and prime input

The functional-equation reflection retains the redundant zeros and has sufficient coefficient mass control. Whole-factor conjugation restores the common orientation.

For \(\kappa<1\), the prime estimate uses a line strictly to the right of \(B\). At \(\kappa=1\), absolute counting supplies the bound. This is not a circular use of the conclusion.

### Centering and induction order

The comparison expression lies in the completed uncentered stage, with the stated affine margins.

The centered difference preserves the same profiles, character, mask, and product of formal scales. This is exactly the structure needed later for cancellation.

### Two Poisson transformations

The first zero frequency is controlled by powerful-product counting.

The first finite transform has the stated Gauss-root normalization. The full residual coprimality condition is removed by Möbius inversion before factorwise estimates.

The sixth-power amplifier’s exceptional column valuations are precisely \(1,6,7\). The pool is disjoint from the live slots under the chosen mesh restriction.

The second correlation transform, width identity (E11), and allowance (E12) check. The inherited divisor is included in the common-support count.

### Nonexceptional children

Deleting slots changes the affine condition by \(6\kappa\) times the deleted length. Hence the cost \(F_{\rm act}/6+\eta\) is the correct one.

The new raw-boundary accounting in (E13a)–(E14) establishes the claimed bounded loss without rounding every slot separately.

### Exceptional children

“Exceptional” here means membership in the finite inducing-character group \(\Theta\), not exclusion of Landau–Siegel zeros.

The valuation restriction supplies the row-count saving in (E15). The deficit identity (E16) checks exactly.

The inequalities in (E17) are proved for the full unbounded multiplicity ranges. They are not justified merely by the finite-field computations.

### Rectangle cancellation

The fixed-lattice expansion has a main coefficient independent of the formal scale and the common norm-twist height. Therefore the two equal-product main terms cancel.

Formal scales are not clipped in this argument. The final divisor extraction is paid for by the displayed raw-reduction inequality.

The maximum in (E19) is indeed \(A-M\). This controls principal children rather than discarding them.

### Mesh and heights

The aggregate support errors are controlled by one \(H_N/\log Z\), with an \(N\)-dependent threshold but a numerical loss structure.

The zero-slot stage precedes the positive-slot stage. Strict children move to lower width bands, and centered comparisons only call a previously completed stage. There is no same-band cycle.

The mesh can therefore be selected before the slot count and arithmetic datum. Constants and height orders may depend on those fixed data without changing the common exponent margin.

The references to longer unchanged finite factorizations are precise imports, not invocations of the baseline’s desired conclusion.

The computer checks are correctly described as supplementary. The essential continuous certificate and analytic arguments are present in the manuscript.

The final distinction between common exponents and target-dependent constants or starting scales is mathematically sufficient. No effectiveness uniform in the target is needed for the continuation contradiction.

## 8. Final determination

The earlier draft was correctly rejected because an essential arithmetic package remained explicitly unproved. That objection no longer applies merely by inheritance: the new appendices provide the missing reflection, norm, coefficient-class, recursion, and uniformity arguments.

After re-reading the full revision, checking the relevant literature hypotheses, inspecting the cited finite-transform dependencies, and independently checking the principal algebraic calculations, I find:

- no remaining arithmetic assumption equivalent to the former \(\mathsf A\);
- no missed principal, imprimitive, or modulus-one case;
- no omission of real exceptional zeros or unrestricted heights;
- no reliance on a preliminary \(11/12\) or \(7/8\) zero-free theorem;
- no replacement of an infinite-range proof by numerical sampling;
- no violation of the current LaTeX contract.

**The revised submission meets the primary target, not merely a conditional or zeta-only secondary target.**

## 2. Sequential audit of the main argument

The following follows the manuscript’s order. Adjacent paragraphs are grouped where they form one calculation or application.

## Section 1 — Result and source dependence

### Constant and strict half-plane

The numerical identity for \(\theta\) is correct, and the constant lies in the required interval. The distinction between
\[
\Re s>\theta
\quad\text{and}\quad
\Re s\ge\theta
\]
is maintained throughout.

### Arithmetic-input paragraph

The listed inputs are the relevant ones. In particular, the proof does not replace the inverse-moment recursion by the low-probe estimate alone: Appendix D uses the broader reflected-energy statement with growing labels and punctures.

The separation of the inverse and plain moment arguments is also important and is respected. The latter is not justified by an inappropriate appeal to the former.

### Uniformity paragraph

The submission correctly distinguishes:

- uniformity of the exponent and slot mesh;
- dependence of constants, derivative orders, and starting scales on fixed arithmetic data.

This is exactly the distinction required for the eventual supremum argument. Uniform starting scales across all conductors are not necessary for that argument.

### Main theorem and attribution paragraph

The theorem states the primary target and a stronger Hecke-family assertion. The later norm-lift argument genuinely supplies the Dirichlet conclusion. The discussion of prior heuristic exploration is appropriately limited and is not used as mathematical evidence.

---

## Section 2 — Geometry, family, and continuation

### Geometry identities

Independent symbolic calculation confirms
\[
l_y-l_x=\frac18,\qquad l_x+l_y=1-\ell,
\]
and, crucially,
\[
\frac{l_x}{2}-1+\frac h6=-\frac{11}{16}.
\]
Thus \(C(s)\) is unchanged by the perturbation, while
\[
C(\theta_\lambda)=\frac{l_x}{2}+\frac b{12}
\]
moves downward by \(\lambda/4\).

This is not merely a relabeling of the old boundary.

### Definition of \(B\)

The supremum includes real zeros, arbitrary heights, and the primitive principal character, while excluding poles. Euler convergence gives the stated upper bound \(B\le1\).

The comments about products and norm twists are adequate: primitive induction preserves the finite-order family, while norm twists translate heights without changing real parts of zeros.

### Continuation criterion: estimates and Mellin transform

The proof correctly obtains
\[
f_\eta(Z)\ll_\eta Z^{C(b_*)},
\qquad
\theta_\lambda<b_*<B.
\]

The estimate near zero is justified by shifting the original integral to arbitrarily large fixed real parts. On those contours the reciprocal Euler product is bounded and the Gaussian controls the vertical integrals.

The resulting Mellin integral converges locally uniformly in \(\Re s>b_*\), including after differentiation.

### Continuation criterion: Fourier identification

The Fourier normalization is correct:
\[
e^{-C(2)u}f_\eta(e^u)
\]
is the inverse Fourier transform of the line-\(2\) integrand, with the usual \(1/(2\pi)\) normalization.

Both required integrability assertions follow from the estimates already established. The meromorphic identity theorem then gives the displayed product identity.

A principal pole creates no difficulty: it can be canceled by a zero of the Mellin transform, whereas a zero of the \(L\)-function would contradict the nonvanishing right-hand side.

The final contradiction does not require the supremum to be attained.

### Imprimitive and principal Dirichlet characters

The finite Euler-factor formula is correct. Every extra factor is a holomorphic unit in \(\Re s>0\), so neither zeros nor their orders are altered there.

The principal-character formula, its positive residue, and the empty product for \(q=1\) are all handled correctly.

### Norm lift and cancellation at one

The split/inert Euler-factor comparison is correct after deletion of primes above \(3q\). Primitive induction of the lift does not change the deleted \(L\)-function.

The possible pole-zero cancellation at \(s=1\) is not overlooked. For the nonprincipal primitive target with principal companion, the displayed positive series for \(L(1,\varepsilon_3)\) is valid.

**Conclusion for Section 2:** The analytic criterion and transfer are rigorous and have the required scope.

---

## Section 3 — Changed low estimate

### Probe definition

The finite modification is applied to the same physical probe, with the original masks and whole-index marks retained. The rescaled and unrescaled prime labels are distinguished correctly.

### Elementary energy lemma

I checked the two branches of the proof.

- In the \(u=z\) branch, the stated error bound \(61r/6\) is below \(11r\).
- In the other branch, the estimate
  \[
  u+t+\frac{2e}{3}\ge \frac y4-\frac{17r}{12}
  \]
  and the positive-part inequality give the claimed \(55r/6\) error.
- The identity reducing the remaining expression to \(5\ell+d-1\) is correct.

The signed slack variables are actually accounted for; they are not silently replaced by nonnegative variables.

### Rescaled lengths and Gram applicability

The lower bounds on \(M'\), \(l_x-d\), and
\[
l_y-d-\frac{11b}{6}
\]
are correct throughout the stated perturbation range. They justify the eventual scale conditions and
\[
P_a^2/Y'\ll P_a^{1/6}.
\]

The row mean squares are explicitly taken over the original bounded row ball. This avoids an unjustified application of an annular kernel estimate to an enlarged domain.

### Joint smooth separation

The profile is separated before row and slot evaluation. Consequently the separated slot coefficients are row-independent.

The Gaussian-annulus treatment and late choice of derivative orders do not introduce a hidden \(Z^{Jr}\) loss.

### Application of general reflected energy

The correspondence with the general energy variables is consistent. The argument uses the general exponent rather than importing the baseline’s specialized zero-loss conclusion outside its original geometry.

The powerful-part count is already included in that input and is not counted a second time.

### Final tuple count

The remaining exponent calculation is correct:
\[
-d+\frac{(5\ell+d-1)_+}{8}\le-\frac{7d}{8},
\]
because \(\ell\le1/5\).

Thus the low estimate is genuinely
\[
Z^{C(\theta_\lambda)+\epsilon},
\]
not \(Z^{C(7/8)+\epsilon}\). This is an essential successful part of the proposed improvement.

---

## Section 4 — Local factors and principal comparison

### Quotient-free correction and high identity

Using the quotient-free selected factors during continuation is appropriate. The finite probe operation does not itself impose an inequality among the lengths.

### Extended principal region

The five local decay exponents are correct at the specified boundaries. The prime-tail calculation gives
\[
336\cdot\frac{225}{4}\cdot(10^{10})^{-4/5}
=\frac{189}{10^6}.
\]
The ensuing product bound implies the asserted contraction.

The larger open region supplies a neighborhood for normal convergence, so holomorphy is not being inferred solely from estimates on a closed boundary.

### Selected-factor cancellation

I independently expanded the displayed identity for \(G_p+H_p\); the residual is exactly zero.

The subsequent estimate for \(G_p/H_p+1\) is legitimate because individual \(H_p\)'s have first been shown bounded away from zero. There is no division by an unproved nonvanishing raw Euler factor.

The restriction of tuple constants to fixed bounded real-part boxes is also correctly stated. This matters later when a very large, but fixed, \(z_\infty\) is selected.

### Prime normalizer

The fixed-ray asymptotic is used only for a fixed extension. An exceptional zero can change the eventual threshold but does not invalidate this asymptotic.

Nonnegative weights are used correctly to turn the termwise selected-factor error into a relative error. No power-saving prime number theorem is needed to obtain the displayed \(O(Z^{-\mu})\) relative correction.

### Principal contours and residues

The contour positions remain inside the established region. The only crossed numerator poles are the specified ones.

The factor \(1/6\) from the pole of \(\zeta_F^S(6z)\) is included in \(c_S\).

The three savings
\[
l_y/20,\qquad h/600,\qquad \mu
\]
are correct. Polynomial arithmetic growth is dominated by decay in the independent coordinates
\[
\Im(s+z),\quad \Im z,\quad \Im w.
\]

The principal-target regularization is appropriate and does not assume the desired zero-free conclusion.
