## Section 5 — Enlarged row envelopes

### Choice of \(\kappa\)

The identity
\[
\kappa=\frac34+2(B-7/8)_+
\]
is correct. It gives the required premise for the positive-slot plain moment when \(\kappa<1\), while the endpoint \(\kappa=1\) uses its unconditional absolute-prime bound.

This avoids assuming the \(11/12\) bootstrap.

### Detector and floor

The detector’s stated range reaches \(a\le1\). The displayed truncated-inverse error is negligible under the chosen height restriction.

The floor bin is correctly separated: it has no required witnessing zero and is counted with \(R=1\). Applying the witness-derived envelope there would have been a genuine error, but the submission does not do so.

### Amplitude bins and greedy selection

The amplitude average and fractional-prefix argument are valid, including the case where all positive-amplitude slots are used. Dropping the fractional final slot loses at most the stated single-slot amount.

Error slots are retained with amplitude zero, so the average is not artificially inflated.

### Moment admissibility and character orientation

The coefficient conversion for the two orientations preserves row independence. Conjugating the whole plain product is the correct operation.

A sufficiently large physical row ramifies at a good prime where every member of \(\Theta\) is unramified. Thus the inducing-character restriction for positive slots is satisfied.

### Capacities and endpoint treatment

The inverse capacity is used with a strict decrement. The second inverse margin follows from
\[
r_*\ge23/37.
\]
The supply requirement \(7/37\) and the plain capacity bound are consistent.

The enlargement from \(\kappa=3/4\) costs at most
\[
\frac{40}{243}\Delta_+<\frac14\Delta_+,
\]
apart from the separately allocated losses.

The choice of \(t\) equating the long and short envelopes gives \(R_*\) as claimed.

The neighborhoods of zero capacity are treated directly using unselected estimates. There is no illegitimate passage to a limiting strict-moment constant.

### Large \(\delta\) and unselected alternative

The envelope \(R=1-\delta\) for \(\delta\ge5/6\) follows from the sign of \((5/6-\delta)(r-1)\).

The unselected alternative \(1-2\delta/3\) comes from the correct crossing at \(r=2/3\).

---

## Section 6 — Nonprincipal comparison

### Holomorphy region and contour order

The stated paths satisfy the inequalities defining \({\cal D}_1(10e)\). In particular, the lower bounds for \(\Re(s+w)\) retain positive slack.

The argument moves the original fixed-bin sum before subdividing according to pointwise amplitudes. It does not attempt to analytically continue an amplitude-bin indicator.

### Tails and joins

The distinction between global bounds and buffered bounds on retained \(s\)-segments is maintained.

The scale exponents and arithmetic height orders are fixed before the external decay order. The explanation of trace tails supplies the missing integration step that would otherwise be easy to overlook.

### Simultaneous conductor allocation

The strict-label conductor saving is correctly multiplicative across the distinct selected primes. For \(j\ge2\),
\[
-\Re w-(j-1)(1/2-\Re w)\le-\frac12.
\]

Thus one numerator conductor allowance pays for the entire strict subset. It is not charged independently in a way that would multiply the global loss by the slot count.

The explicit \(12e\) loss is retained rather than disguised as an arbitrarily small loss at fixed \(e\).

### Exponent \(E_\lambda\)

Combining the outside Mellin powers, row count, numerator allowance, and prime amplitudes gives the displayed \(E_\lambda\). I found no missing normalization power in this calculation.

### Small rows

The global lines lie in the asserted holomorphy region. The local selected-factor bounds suffice after divisor counting.

The resulting small-row exponent has a substantial positive saving relative to \(C(B)\). Unit rows and possible principal denominators are not omitted.

### Unbounded large rows

The tail exponent is correct. With the stated fixed choices of \(\zeta\) and \(z_\infty\), it is below \(-1\) throughout the perturbation range.

The size of \(z_\infty\) affects constants and test orders, not the logical order of the subsequent height choice. This is a valid treatment of an unbounded row range, not a finite-range computation.

---

## Section 7 — Exact certificate and closure

### Continuous endpoint certificate

Independent symbolic expansion gives **zero residual** for the polynomial identity.

Its positivity argument covers the entire rectangle:
\[
0\le y\le\frac12,\qquad 0\le\delta\le\frac56.
\]
In particular, \(9-13y\ge5/2\), so the final bracket is nonnegative.

The bounds on \({\cal J}\), together with \(v\le17(3+5y)\), give the claimed exact margin.

### Perturbation

The identity
\[
E_\lambda(h;\delta,x\delta,R_*)-E_0^*
=\lambda\!\left[-\frac14+(1+x)\delta+\frac32R_*\right]
\]
checks exactly.

The resulting margins are
\[
m_*=\frac{59873}{550800000},
\]
and
\[
m_*-2\cdot10^{-6}
=\frac{293857}{2754000000}
>\frac1{10000}.
\]

These are rational certificates, not grid observations.

### Other endpoints

The floor, short-row, and large-\(\delta\) formulas all agree with direct substitution into \(E_\lambda\). The stated signs of their \(d\)-slopes are correct.

Subtracting \(B-\theta_\lambda\) correctly absorbs the extra \(h\Delta_+/4\) term. Both \(B<7/8\) and \(B\ge7/8\) are handled.

The supply check extends over the whole enlarged interval, including \(h<d\le h+\zeta\).

### Order of choices

The order is coherent:

1. moment losses and capacity decrement;
2. target-independent mesh;
3. fixed slot system;
4. principal and remaining contour losses;
5. target-specific arithmetic data and height orders;
6. \(\tau\);
7. external tail order \(N\).

The potentially tiny value of \(\mu\) is not an obstruction. Only positivity and target independence of the eventual saving are needed; there is no necessary inequality \(\lambda/4<\mu\).

### Final application of the criterion

The low exponent uses \(\omega=(B-\theta)/2\). The high comparison obtains a common positive saving, while constants and thresholds may depend on the target.

Neither comparison function depends on the auxiliary height cutoff. This supplies the actual hypotheses of the continuation criterion and closes the contradiction.

## 3. Sequential audit of the arithmetic appendices

## Appendix A — Conventions and smooth uniformity

- **Arithmetic package and dependency diagram:** The diagram is acyclic. The plain moment’s use of \(B\) is confined to estimates to the right of \(B\); it does not import the target conclusion.
- **Primary generators and additive character:** The conventions are compatible with the Eisenstein lattice. The self-duality assertion follows from the two stated additive tests.
- **Zero extensions:** Retaining zeros for zero and negative character powers is essential and is consistently used in later mark and collision identities.
- **Quadratic phases:** The Gaussian calculation fixes the finite phase rather than leaving an unspecified Gauss-sum unit.
- **Gauss–Jacobi identities:** The prime identities and their squarefree CRT extensions have the correct normalizations and phases.
- **Fixed numerator families:** Reduction modulo sixth powers and the Kummer/Artin construction yield a finite fixed family. Moving good primes are not improperly absorbed into the fixed datum.
- **Smooth separation:** Euler differentiation avoids derivative-order powers of the large scale. The common Fourier density is chosen before row evaluation.
- **Propagation of orders:** Backward selection of seminorm and height orders is valid once finite depth and uniform child profiles have been established; those additional conditions are addressed in the later appendices.

No essential gap was found here.

## Appendix B — Completed reflection

### Source coefficients and masks

The conjugation rule uses \(-\mu\), not \(\mu\), and matches the source cusp table. The support includes shared primes of \(n,b\) and does not impose an unsupported dual coprimality condition.

The prime above \(2\) is handled through the cubic coefficient, without pretending that it is a good sextic prime.

The primal mask acts on the whole completed index, as required.

### Finite Fourier table and cusp sectors

The four cases in (B6) are correct, including the zero-exponent/nonunit-frequency cases.

## Appendix E — Plain fourth moment

### Inductive class, masks, and reflection

### Induction order and centering

The zero-slot bands are completed before the positive-slot bands. Within a width band, the uncentered range is completed before the centered comparison calls it.

The comparison estimates place that call inside the established range. Thus this is not a same-width circular induction.

The fixed-lattice main coefficient is independent of both the scale and the twist height after the extracted norm powers cancel.

The equal products of scales therefore cancel the two main terms exactly. The all-scale bound covers small formal scales rather than silently assuming they are large.

### Slot-count independence and heights

Aggregate subset errors are controlled by \(H_N/\log Z\), with the threshold allowed to depend on \(N\). This is different from paying a fixed exponent loss for every slot.

The mesh and real loss budget are selected independently of slot count and arithmetic datum; the constants and analytic orders may depend on them.

The strict width decrease and the non-amplification of amplifier errors give finite depth. Backward propagation then supplies the required fixed polynomial height dependence.

## 5. Remaining comments and final disposition

I have only nonessential presentation comments:

1. The reuse of \(b_*\) for a real boundary inside the continuation lemma and for fixed arithmetic data later is scoped correctly but unnecessarily confusing.
2. The alternation between physical scales and logarithmic lengths in notation such as \(S_\psi\) could be made more explicit.
3. A compact table of the inherited coefficient classes would make the long dependency audit easier to navigate.
4. The publicly claimed source hashes should ideally be accompanied by the actual downloadable source manifest/bundle, since the present review could not independently reproduce those byte-level checks.

None of these comments requires a new mathematical argument or changes the conclusion of this audit.

**Final disposition:** The perturbation is supported by a changed low estimate, admissible enlarged row envelopes, extended contour regions, exact positive margins, and a consistent uniform order of choices. The continuation argument then yields the claimed strict half-plane for every Dirichlet character, including principal and imprimitive characters and the character modulo one. Real exceptional zeros and arbitrary heights are included; boundary zeros are not excluded.
